GPIO_PULLSELn 

GPIO pull-up/down selection. Only valid if the corresponding GPIO_PULLEN is high. 

0 GPIO_PULLSELs pull-down is selected. 

1 GPIO_PULLSELs pull-up is selected.

<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">CLK_MODE7</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">0</td></tr></table>

CLK_MODE7 Selects the CLKO7 output source 

0 sfc_ck_div 2 (65MHz, 52MHz, 39MHz, etc.) 

1 f26m_ck (26MHz) 

2 emi1x_ck_div2 (65MHz, 52MHz, etc.) 

3 command d2d_ck_div2 (37.5MHz, 26MHz, etc.) 

4 f32k_ck (32kHz) 

5 command d2d_ck (65MHz, 52MHz, 26MHz) 

6 BT ahb_bus_clk_div2 (65MHz, 52MHz, etc.) 

7 MMsys_emi1x_ck_div2 (65MHz, 52MHz) 

8 MCUSYS ahb bus clk (65MHz, 52MHz) 

9 MSDC clk_div2 (~49MHz) 

A ARM clk_div4 (65MHz, 52MHz, etc.) 

B FM 128Khz clk (128kHz) 

C USB 48Mhz clk (48MHz) 

D x4g md2g clk (52MHz) 

E BSI_clk_div2 (65MHz) 

F DSP_clk_div2 (78MHz) 

## GPIO+0x4h GPIO xxx register SET

## GPIO_XXX_SET

For all registers addresses listed above, writing to the +4h address offset will perform a bit-wise OR function between the 16-bit written value and the 16-bit register value already existing in the corresponding GPIO_xxx registers. 

Example: 

If GPIO_DIR0 (GPIO+0000h) = 16’h0F0F, 

writing GPIO_DIR0_SET (GPIO+0004h) = 16’F0F0 will result in GPIO_DIR0 = 16’hFFFF. 

## GPIO+0x8h GPIO xxx register CLR

GPIO_XXX_CLR 

For all registers addresses listed above, writing to the +8h address offset will perform a bit-wise AND-NOT function between the 16-bit written value and the 16-bit register value already existing in the corresponding GPIO_xxx registers. 

Example: 

If GPIO_DIR0 (GPIO+0000h) = 16’h0F0F, 

writing GPIO_DIR0_CLR (GPIO+0008h) = 16’0F0F will result in GPIO_DIR0 = 16’h0000.

## 4.5 General-purpose Timer

## 4.5.1 General Description

Four general-purpose timers are provided. Three timers are 16 bits long and one timer is 32 bits long. Each runs independently. GPT1 ~ 3 use 32k clock source to count, whereas GPT4 uses 26M clock source. The 26M clock source can be gated when the system enters the sleep mode, and this will cause GPT4 to stop counting, whereas 32k clock source is always toggling. GPT1 and GPT2 can operate in one of the two modes: one-shot mode and auto-repeat mode. GPT3 and GPT4 are free running timer. In the one-shot mode, when the timer counts down and reaches 0, it will be halted. In the auto-repeat mode, when the timer reaches 0, it will simply be reset to counting down the initial value and repeating the count-down to 0. This loop keeps repeating until the disabling signal is set to 1. Regardless of the timer’s mode, if the countdown initial value (i.e. GPTIMER1_DAT for GPT1 or GPTIMER_DAT2 for GPT2) is written when the timer is running, the new initial value will not take effect until the next time the timer is restarted. In the auto-repeat mode, the new countdown start value is 

used on the next countdown iteration. Therefore, before enabling the timer, the desired values for GPTIMER_DAT and the GPTIMER_PRESCALER registers must first be set.

<table><tr><td>Type</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>0</td><td>0</td></tr></table>

EN Controls GPT4 to start counting or to stop 

0 Disable GPT4 

1 Enable GPT4 

LOCK Controls GPT4 EN bit can be modified or not. If LOCK = 0, EN can be modified. If LOCK = 1, the EN value will be fixed, and the LOCK bit will always be 1 and cannot be modified until hardware reset. 

0 Unlock 

1 Lock

CNT [31:0] If EN=1, GPT4 will be a free running timer. This register records the GPT4 value. If EN = 0, this register will be cleared to 0. This register does not allow continuous read. It requires at least one 26M clock cycle between two APB reads. 


## 4.6 MCU OSTIMER

## 4.6.1 Overview

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-20/a0b5e3cf-5739-46d1-96ae-050c03350f29/34c0667b49fd157e870a46e45c08660efaad12e0fadf9d1a9d01aed7f33af655.jpg)



Figure 37. OS timer system view


The OS timer is a hardware timer which specifies the OS time frame duration and generates time-out interrupts by programming the frame counter number. The OS timer has the pause mode. The user can specify pause duration period before the pause mode, and the timer will resume from the pause mode by the external wakeup sources or when the pause duration is timed out. 

## 4.6.2 Terminology

## 4.6.3

## Introduction to Wakeup Source

The OS timer only accepts level trigger wakeup source. The wakeup sources are all treated as asynchronous input (will be changed) and will be synchronized by OST clock in OST wakeup source controller. 

The possible wakeup sources are listed in the table below. 

<table><tr><td>No</td><td>Wakeup source</td></tr><tr><td>0</td><td>GPT</td></tr><tr><td>1</td><td>EINT</td></tr><tr><td>2</td><td>Timer trigger</td></tr><tr><td>3</td><td>KP</td></tr><tr><td>4</td><td>MSDC</td></tr><tr><td>5</td><td>ANALOG</td></tr><tr><td>6</td><td>DSP</td></tr><tr><td>7</td><td>GPI</td></tr><tr><td>8</td><td>Reserved</td></tr><tr><td>9</td><td>Reserved</td></tr><tr><td>10</td><td>Reserved</td></tr><tr><td>11</td><td>Reserved</td></tr><tr><td>12</td><td>DSP_ASYNC</td></tr><tr><td>13</td><td>GPI_ASYNC</td></tr><tr><td>14</td><td>Reserved</td></tr><tr><td>15</td><td>Reserved</td></tr><tr><td>16</td><td>Reserved</td></tr><tr><td>17</td><td>Reserved</td></tr><tr><td>18</td><td>Reserved</td></tr></table>

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-20/a0b5e3cf-5739-46d1-96ae-050c03350f29/75aeaf789924dd40f77d8f3b2ba65713bf0a3687ac1f20c13a2fc858ec8ae464.jpg)



Figure 38. Wakeup event and Irq_b integration diagram


wakeup_irq_b may be asserted before wakeup_event_b is asserted from the wakeup source peripheral to ensure the software will enter wakeup ISR after the pause command is set. 

Recommended software programming sequence: 

1. I-BIT is set when a pause command is executed. 

2. Set up IRQ mask registers to select IRQ wakeup sources 

3. Pause criteria are met. 

4. Set up pause command. 

5. Confirm the pause command is executed at OST, and check if the pause command is pending or not. 

6. Set up processor in request for interrupt state. 

7. The processor clock will be off if there is no interrupt. 

8. Check if the pause request command is completed (after the processor clock is active or resumes). 

9. Clear I-BIT.

<table><tr><td>Address</td><td>Name</td><td>Width</td><td>Register function</td></tr><tr><td>0XA01F0000</td><td>OST_CON</td><td>32</td><td>OS timer control registerOnly updated when OST_CMD.OST_WR is set and OST_STA.WR_RDY is high.</td></tr><tr><td>0XA01F0004</td><td>OST_CMD</td><td>32</td><td>OS timer commandOnly valid when the write data BIT31 to BIT16 is 0x6276.</td></tr><tr><td>0XA01F0008</td><td>OST_STA</td><td>32</td><td>OS timer status</td></tr><tr><td>0XA01F000C</td><td>OST_FRM</td><td>32</td><td>OS timer frame durationSpecifies OS timer frame duration by micron second resolution. The maximum duration is 8ms and minimum duration is 1ms. This register should be set before OS timer is enabled. The hardware will set up OST_ISR[0] periodically at frame duration period when the OS timer is enabled.</td></tr><tr><td>0XA01F0010</td><td>OST_FRM_F32K</td><td>32</td><td>OS timer frame duration by 32K clockSpecifies OS timer frame duration with 30.5176 micron second (32kHz) resolution. The maximum duration is 8ms and minimum duration is 1ms. This register should be set before the OS timer is enabled.OST_FRAM_F32K*30.5176us should be less than OST_FRM_NUM*OST_FRM*1us - 30.5176us.Set up OST_FRM_NUM if the frame duration is shorter than system settling time.</td></tr><tr><td>0XA01F0014</td><td>OST_UFN</td><td>32</td><td>OS timer un-alignment frame numberSpecifies OS timer un-alignment event frame number count. This register value is updated to OS timer only when OST_CMD.OST_WR is set. The read value of this register may not be the current OS time Un-alignment frame number. The hardware will count down the OST_UFN counter at frame time-out and stop the count-down when the current value is 0. When the OS timer is in the pause mode, this counter is still active and wakes up the OS timer when OST_UFN becomes 0 at frame time-out boundary.The software should enable the OS timer pause mode when OST_UFN is larger than 1, or the pause command will be ignored by the hardware.</td></tr><tr><td>0XA01F0018</td><td>OST_AFN</td><td>32</td><td>OS timer alignment frame numberSpecifies OS timer alignment event frame number count. This register value is updated to the OS timer only when OST_CMD.OST_WR is set. The read value of this register may not be the current OS time alignment frame number. The hardware will count down the OST_AFN counter at frame time-out and stop the count-down when the current value is 0. The hardware will set OST_ISR[1] when OST_AFN is 1 or 0 at frame time-out, and the OS timer is in normal mode. OST_AFN is current hardware AFN down counter value, and the software cannot read this register directly. The software can only read the current frame number through the OST_AFN_R register, and there is synchronization latency from OST_AFN to OST_AFN_R.</td></tr><tr><td>0XA01F001C</td><td>OST_AFN_DLY</td><td>32</td><td>OS timer alignment frame delay number countSpecifies the OS timer alignment event frame delay count due to OS timer pause mode. The software has to use the OST_CMD.OST_RD command to update this register value, or the value may be out of date.</td></tr><tr><td>0XA01F0020</td><td>OST_UFN_R</td><td>32</td><td>Current OS timer un-alignment frame numberSpecifies the OS timer current un-alignment frame number. The software has to use the OST_CMD.OST_RD command to update this register value, or the value may be out of date.</td></tr><tr><td>0XA01F0024</td><td>OST_AFN_R</td><td>32</td><td>Current OS timer alignment frame numberSpecifies the OS timer current alignment frame number. The software has to use the OST_CMD.OST_RD command to update this register value, or the value may be out of date.</td></tr><tr><td>0XA01F0030</td><td>OST_INT_MASK</td><td>32</td><td>OS timer interrupt maskSpecifies the OS timer interrupt mask control.</td></tr><tr><td>0XA01F0040</td><td>OST_ISR</td><td>32</td><td>OS timer interrupt statusSpecifies the OS timer interrupt status. The software has to write 1 at the corresponding bit to clear the interrupt status bit. OSR_ISR[2-0] are also cleared when the OSR_WR command is executed and AFN, UFN are updated.</td></tr><tr><td>0XA01F0050</td><td>OST_EVENT_MASK</td><td>32</td><td>OS timer event maskSpecifies which wakeup event will be masked to wake up the OS timer in the OS timer pause mode.</td></tr><tr><td>0XA01F0054</td><td>OST_WAKEUP_STA</td><td>32</td><td>OS timer event wakeup status</td></tr><tr><td>0XA01F0060</td><td>OST_DBG_WAKEUP</td><td>32</td><td>OS timer debug wakeup</td></tr></table>

Overview This register specifies the OS timer frame duration with 30.5176 micron second (32kHz) resolution. The maximum duration is 8ms and minimum duration is 1ms. This register should be set up before the OS timer is enabled. OST_FRAM_F32K*30.5176us should be less than OST_FRM_NUM*OST_FRM*1us - 30.5176us. Set up OST_FRM_NUM if the frame duration is shorter than the system settling time.

## Overview

This register specifies the OS timer un-alignment event frame number count. This register value is updated to the OS timer only when OST_CMD.OST_WR is set. The read value of this register may not be the current OS time un-alignment frame number. The hardware will count down the OST_UFN counter at frame time-out and stop the count-down when the current value is 0. When the OS timer is in the pause mode, this counter is still active and wakes up the OS timer when OST_UFN becomes 0 at frame time-out boundary. The software should enable the OS timer pause mode when OST_UFN is larger than 1, or the pause command will be ignored by hardware. 

<table><tr><td>Bit(s)</td><td>Name</td><td>Description</td></tr><tr><td>31:0</td><td>OST_UFN</td><td></td></tr></table>

## 0XA01F0018


OS timer alignment frame number



OST_AFN


<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>31</td><td>30</td><td>29</td><td>28</td><td>27</td><td>26</td><td>25</td><td>24</td><td>23</td><td>22</td><td>21</td><td>20</td><td>19</td><td>18</td><td>17</td><td>16</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td colspan="16">OST_AFN[31:16]</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td colspan="16">RW</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td colspan="16">OST_AFN[15:0]</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td colspan="16">RW</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td></tr></table>

Overview This register specifies the OS timer alignment event frame number count. This register value is updated to the OS timer only when OST_CMD.OST_WR is set. The read value of this register may not be the current OS time alignment frame number. The hardware will count down the OST_AFN counter at frame time-out and stop the count-down when the current value is 0. The hardware will set up OST_ISR[1] when OST_AFN is 1 or 0 at frame time-out and the OS timer is in the normal mode. OST_AFN is the current hardware AFN down counter value, and the software cannot read this register directly. The software can only read the current frame number through the OST_AFN_R register, and there is synchronization latency from OST_AFN to OST_AFN_R. 

<table><tr><td>Bit(s)</td><td>Name</td><td>Description</td></tr><tr><td>31:0</td><td>OST_AFN</td><td></td></tr></table>

## 4.7 UART

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-20/a0b5e3cf-5739-46d1-96ae-050c03350f29/78d0ea513b1dd974ad6fec37a6e294beb89d492ad1b148a3cc7b096e7398db34.jpg)


## 4.7.1 General Description

The baseband chipset houses three UARTs. The UARTs provide full duplex serial communication channels between baseband chipset and external devices. 

UART supports word length from 5 to 8 bits, an optional parity bit and one or two stop bits, and is fully programmable. A 16-bit programmable baud rate generator is included, together with separate transmit and receive FIFOs. A diagnostic loopback mode is provided. UART also includes two pairs of DMA handshake lines used to indicate when the FIFOs are ready to transfer data to or receive data from the CPU. Interrupts can be generated from any of the 10 sources. 

After hardware reset, the UART will be in the M16C450 mode. Its FIFOs can be enabled and the UART can then enter the M16550A mode. The UART has further additional functions beyond the M16550A mode. Each of the extended functions can be selected individually under software control. 

The UART provides more powerful enhancements than the industry-standard 16550: 

Hardware flow control: This feature is very useful when the ISR latency is hard to predict and control in the embedded applications. The MCU is relieved of having to fetch the received data within a fixed amount of time. 

· High-speed mode: For high-speed external devices, the UART supports up to 3.25M baud rate. 

· Low power: To save power, the UART supports dynamic clock change while keeping the same baud rate. 

Note: In order to enable any of the enhancements, the Enhanced Mode bit, EFR[4] must be set. If EFR[4] is not set, IER[7:5], FCR[5:4], IIR[5:4] and MCR[7:6] cannot be written. The Enhanced Mode bit ensures that the UART is backward compatible with the software that has been written for 16C450 and 16550A devices. 


Figure 41 shows the block diagram of the UART device.


![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-20/a0b5e3cf-5739-46d1-96ae-050c03350f29/69916b8f7660f6e56e48d725387e811fcd36a02af93a37f2e925c61f896043c2.jpg)



Figure 41. Block diagram of UART


## 4.7.2 Log Accelerator Description

In order to download more information from the network and to reduce the software loading, the software only writes all information at the specified external memory and triggers the hardware to transmit it though UART to the PC client. 

Before transmitting data through the UART, the software needs to specify some settings. At first, the address of layer 1 and PS data are required. The amount of data is so huge that the data are usually located in the external memory. The software needs to specify the starting address and the size of the ring buffer. However, it is acceptable if the address is located in the internal RAM (in the same layer of the AHB bus). 

Besides the location of the original data, the software also needs to specify the target address. There are two methods to transmit data to the UART. 

1. The log_acc module writes all encoded data to the specified memory and issues an interrupt to the MCU after the entire block is transmitted. Next, the software uses the DMA to move the data to the UART or USB. 

2. The log_acc module writes the UART port directly. 

With method 1, the software needs to set up a specified memory to store all encoded data. When the ARM receives the IRQ signal from LOG_ACC, the software can start to use DMA to move the data to the UART or USB. This method has limitation: If the encoded data are not word-aligned, the software should separate the encoded data into two parts. One part is sent by “DMA size=word”, and the remaining not-word-aligned data are sent by “DMA size=byte”. If the software uses “DMA size=byte” to send all data, the bus performance will be degraded, and the transmission rate will be too slow. 

With method 2, LOG_ACC will write the UART port directly by the AHB single mode. The software needs to enable this function in the register and set the target address to UART port. The software should also set up the appreciated threshold value in “UARTn_FCR”; otherwise log_acc will write too many data to UART FIFO. Although log_acc can detect the status of the UART, it may not be the first master to access the UART while the UART FIFO is safe to send. If too many data occur, the software can try to use the DMA approach since this approach will hold the transmission until the FIFO is not full. If the software can guarantee that one UART is dedicated to the LOG_ACC module, there will not be such problem. 

The log_acc module writes data to the UART TX FIFO directly. 

At a specific timing, only one user of VFIFO/LOGACC can access the UART Tx channel. When the Tx channel is switched from LOGACC to VFIFO, the following conditions should be met: 

FIFO enable 

Tx DMA enable 

Reset Tx FIFO 

When the Tx channel is switched from VFIFO to LOGACC, the following conditions should be met: 

FIFO enable 

Tx DMA disable 

Reset Tx FIFO

Table 55. IIR[5:0] codes associated with the possible interrupts


## Line status interrupt

A Rx line status Interrupt (IIR[5:0`] = 000110b) is generated if ELSI (IER[2]) is set and any of BI, FE, PE or OE (LSR[4:1]) is set. The interrupt is cleared by reading the line status register. 

## Rx data received interrupt

A Rx received interrupt (IER[5:0] = 000100b) is generated if EFRBI (IER[0]) is set and either Rx data are placed in the Rx buffer register or the Rx trigger level is reached. The interrupt is cleared by reading the Rx buffer register or the Rx FIFO (if enabled). 

## Rx data time-out interrupt

When virtual FIFO mode is disabled, the Rx data time-out interrupt will be generated if all of the following conditions are applied: 

1. FIFO contains at least one character. 

2. The most recent character is received longer than four character periods ago (including all start, parity and stop bits). 

3. The most recent CPU read of the FIFO was longer than four character periods ago. 

The time-out timer is restarted upon receipt of a new byte from the Rx shift register or on a CPU read from the Rx FIFO. 

The Rx data time-out interrupt is enabled by setting EFRBI (IER[0]) to 1 and is cleared by reading Rx FIFO. 

When virtual FIFO mode is enabled, the Rx data time-out interrupt will be generated if all of the following conditions are applied: 

1. FIFO is empty. 

2. The most recent character is received longer than four character periods ago (including all start, parity and stop bits); 

3. The most recent CPU read of the FIFO was longer than four character periods ago. 

The time-out timer is restarted on receipt of a new byte from the Rx shift register. 

## Rx holding register empty interrupt

A Tx holding register empty interrupt (IIR[5:0] = 000010b) is generated if ETRBI (IER[1]) is set and either the Tx holding register or, if FIFOs are enabled, the Tx FIFO becomes empty. The interrupt is cleared by writing to the Tx holding register or Tx FIFO if FIFO is enabled. 

## Modem status change interrupt

A modem status change interrupt (IIR[5:0] = 000000b) is generated if EDSSI (IER[3]) is set and either DDCD, TERI, DDSR or DCTS (MSR[3:0]) is set. The interrupt is cleared by reading the modem status register. 

## Software flow control interrupt

A software flow control interrupt (IIR[5:0] = 010000b) is generated if the software flow control is enabled and XOFFI (IER[5]) is set, indicating that an XOFF character has been received. The interrupt is cleared by reading the interrupt identification register. 

## Hardware flow control interrupt

A hardware flow control interrupt (IER[5:0] = 100000b) is generated if the hardware flow control is enabled and either RTSI (IER[6]) or CTSI (IER[7]) is set, indicating that a rising edge has been detected on either of RTS/CTS modem control line. The interrupt is cleared by reading the interrupt identification register.

<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>FIFOERR</td><td>TEMT</td><td>THRE</td><td>BI</td><td>FE</td><td>PE</td><td>OE</td><td>DR</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="8">R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>0</td><td>1</td><td>1</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td></tr></table>


Line status register. Modified when LCR[7] = 0. 


FIFOERR Rx FIFO error indicator 

0 No PE, FE, BI set in Rx FIFO 

1 Set to 1 when there is at least one PE, FE or BI in Rx FIFO. 

## TEMT EMT

Tx holding register (or Tx FIFO) and the Tx shift register are empty. 

0 Empty conditions below are not met. 

1 If FIFOs are enabled, the bit is set whenever Tx FIFO and the Tx shift register are empty. If FIFOs are disabled, the bit will be set whenever the Tx holding register and Tx shift register are empty. 

## THRE

Indicates if there is room for Tx holding register or Tx FIFO is reduced to its trigger level. 

0 Reset whenever the contents of Tx FIFO are more than its trigger level (FIFOs are enabled), or whenever the Tx holding register is not empty(FIFOs are disabled). 

1 Set whenever the contents of Tx FIFO are reduced to its trigger level (FIFOs are enabled), or whenever the Tx holding register is empty and ready to accept new data (FIFOs are disabled). 

## BI

## Break interrupt

0 Reset by the CPU reading this register 

1 If the FIFOs are disabled, this bit will be set whenever SIN is held in the 0 state for more than one transmission time (START bit + DATA bits + PARITY + STOP bits). If the FIFOs are enabled, this error will be associated with a corresponding character in FIFO and is flagged when this byte is at the top of the FIFO. When a break occurs, only one zero character will be loaded into the FIFO. The next character transfer is enabled when SIN goes into the marking state and receives the next valid start bit. 

## FE

## Framing error

0 Reset by the CPU reading this register 

1 If the FIFOs are disabled, this bit will be set if the received data do not have a valid byte it refers to is the next to be read. 

## PE

Parity error 

0 Reset by the CPU reading this register 

MediaTek Confidential 

1 If the FIFOs are disabled, this bit will be set if the received data do not have a valid parity bit. If the FIFOs are enabled, the state of this bit will be revealed when the referred byte is the next to be read. 

## Overrun error

0 Reset by the CPU reading this register. 

1 If the FIFOs are disabled, this bit will be set if the Rx buffer is not read by the CPU before the new data from the Rx shift register overwrite the previous contents. If the FIFOs are enabled, an overrun error will occur when Rx FIFO is full and the Rx shift register becomes full. OE is set as soon as this happens. The character in the shift register is then overwritten but not transferred to the FIFO. 

## DR Data ready

0 Cleared by the CPU reading the Rx buffer or by reading all the FIFO bytes. 

1 Set by the Rx buffer becoming full or by a byte being transferred into the FIFO.

UARTn_MS

Note: After a reset, D4-D7 are inputs. A modem status interrupt can be cleared by writing ‘0’ or set by writing ‘1’ to this register. D0-D3 can be written to. Modified when LCR[7] = 0. 

CTS Clear to send 

When $\mathsf { L o o p } = " 0 " ,$ , this value will be the complement of the NCTS input signal. 

When $\mathsf { L o o p } = " 1 "$ , this value will be equal to the RTS bit in the modem control register. 

## DCTS Delta clear to send

0 Cleared if the state of CTS has not changed since this register was last read. 

Set if the state of CTS has changed since this register was last read.

Note: Only when LCR = BF’h 

AUTO CTS Enables hardware transmission flow control 0 Disable Enable 

AUTO RTS Enables hardware reception flow control 0 Disable Enable 

LOWER_RATEFIX Enables ratefix function for lower clock 0 Disable 1 Enable 

ENABLE-E Enables enhancement features 0 Disable 1 Enable 

Software flow control bits 00xx No Tx flow control 10xx Transmit XON1/XOFF1 as flow control bytes 

MediaTek Confidential 

01xx Transmit XON2/XOFF2 as flow control bytes 

11xx Transmit XON1 & XON2 and XOFF1 & XOFF2 as flow control words 

xx00 No Rx flow control 

xx10 Receive XON1/XOFF1 as flow control bytes 

xx01 Receive XON2/XOFF2 as flow control bytes 

xx11 Receive XON1 & XON2 and XOFF1 & XOFF2 as flow control words 

Note: When 11xx or xx11, ESC character is not supported and ESC_EN must be 0. 

In MT6250, UART only supports 52MHz, 26MHz and 13MHz.

UART sample counter base

## Selects frequency

0 When in 52MHz clock domain

## RATE_FIX

When "rate_fix"(34H[0]) is set, you can transmit and receive data only if f13m_en is enable and freq_sel (34H[2]) is set to 1, or f26m_en is enabled and freq_sel (34H[2]) is set to 0. 

## AUTOBAUD_RATE_FIX

When "autobaud_rate_fix"(34H[1]) is set, Tx/Rx the autobaud packet only if 

0 f13m_en is enabled and freq_sel (34H[2]) is set to 1, or 

1 f26m_en is enabled and freq_sel (34H[2]) is set to 0. 

## FREQ_SEL

Selects frequency 

0 Select f26m_en for rate_fix and autobaud_rate_fix 

1 Select f13m_en for rate_fix and autobaud_rate_fix

## SLEEP_EN For sleep mode issue

0 Does not deal with sleep mode indication signal 

1 Activate hardware flow control or software control according to software initial settings when the chip enters the sleep mode. Release hardware flow when the chip wakes up. However, for software control, UART sends xon when awaken and when FIFO does not reach threshold level.

## TO_CNT_AUTORST Time-out counter auto reset register

0 After Rx time-out takes place, the software shall reset the interrupt by reading UART 0x4C. 

1 The time-out counter will be auto reset. Set this register when Rain’s new DMA is used. 

## TX_DMA_EN

TX_DMA mechanism enable signal 

0 Does not use DMA in Tx. 

1 Use DMA in Tx. When this register is enabled, the flow control is based on the DMA threshold and generates a time-out interrupt for DMA. 

## RX_DMA_EN

RX_DMA mechanism enable signal 

0 Does not use DMA in Rx 

1 Use DMA in Rx. When this register is enabled, the flow control is based on the DMA threshold and generates a time-out interrupt.

## 4.8 I2C/SCCB Controller

## 4.8.1 General Description

I2C (Inter-IC)/SCCB (Serial Camera Control Bus) is a two-wire serial interface. The two signals are SCL and SDA. SCL is a clock signal driven by the master. SDA is a bi-directional data signal that can be driven by either the master or the slave. This generic controller supports the master role and conforms to the I2C specification. 

## 4.8.1.1 Features

 I2C compliant master mode operation 

 Adjustable clock speed for LS/FS mode operation 

 Supports 7-bit/10-bit addressing 

 Supports high-speed mode 

 Supports slave clock extension 

 START/STOP/REPEATED START condition 

 Manual transfer mode 

 Multi-write per transfer (up to 8 data bytes) 

 Multi-read per transfer(up to 8 data bytes ) 

 Multi-transfer per transaction Combined format transfer with length change capability 

 Active drive/wired-and I/O configuration 

## 4.8.1.2 Manual Transfer Mode

The controller offers manualtransfer modes 

In addition to the slave address register, the controller has a built-in 8-byte deep FIFO which allows the MCU to prepare for up to 8 bytes of data for a write transfer, or read up to 8 bytes of data for a read transfer. 

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-20/a0b5e3cf-5739-46d1-96ae-050c03350f29/9aedb601a01e4147d0a6440f3c7ca96fdb2170bc9b7b3949dcdabbc21d8ba3e9.jpg)


## 4.8.1.3 Transfer Format Support

This controller is designed to be as generic as possible in order to support a wide range of devices that may utilize different combinations of transfer formats. Here are the transfer formats that can be supported through different software configurations: 

## Wording convention notes

Transfer = Anything encapsulated within a Start and Stop or Repeated Start 

Transfer length = Number of bytes within the transfer 

Transaction = This is the top unit. Everything combined equals 1 transaction. 

Transaction length = Number of transfers to be conducted 

Master to slave dir 

Slave to master dir

SLAVE_ADDR [7:0] 

Specifies the slave address of the device to be accessed. Bit 0 is defined by the I2C protocol as a bit that indicates the direction of transfer. 1 = master read, 0 = master write. 

8h 

INTR_MASK 

<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>TIME OUT</td><td></td><td>HS_N ACKER</td><td>ACKERR</td><td>TRA NSA C_C OMP</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>R.W</td><td></td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>0</td><td></td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td></tr></table>

This register provides masks for the corresponding interrupt sources as indicated in the intr_stat register. 

1 = Allow interrupt 

0 = Disable interrupt 

Note: While disabled, the corresponding interrupt will not be asserted. However, intr_stat will still be updated with the status, i.e. the mask does not affect intr_stat register values. 

I2CREG+000 

Ch 


Interrupt status register



INTR_STAT


<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>TIME OUT</td><td></td><td>HS_N ACK ERR</td><td>ACK ERR</td><td>TRA NSA C_C OMP</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>W1C</td><td></td><td>W1C</td><td>W1C</td><td>W1C</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>0</td><td></td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td></tr></table>

When an interrupt is issued by the I2C controller, this register will need to be read by the MCU to determine the cause for the interrupt. After this status is read and appropriate actions are taken, the corresponding interrupt source will need to be written to 1 to clear. 

TIMEOUT 

This status is asserted if time-out is enabled and the timer expires. The internal master state machine will stop, and the MCU will need to manually clear the 

state machine by either issuing software reset by disabling the transact_en bit. The time-out can be used to detect PCB or I2C malfunction. 

## HS_NACKERR

This status is asserted if the HS master code NACK error detection is enabled. If enabled, the HS master code NACK ERR will cause transaction to end, and stop will be issued. 

ACKERR This status is asserted if ACK error detection is enabled. If enabled,ACKERR will cause the transaction to end, and stop will be issued. 

TRANSAC_COMP This status is asserted when a transaction has completed successfully. 

## I2CREG+001

## 0h

## Control register

## CONTROL

<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>TIME OUT_EN</td><td>RESET_BUS_BUSY_EN</td><td>TRANSFER_LEN_CHANGE</td><td>ACKERR_DET_EN</td><td>DIR_CHANGE</td><td>CLK_EXT_EN</td><td>DMA_EN</td><td>RS_STOP</td><td></td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>RW</td><td>RW</td><td>RW</td><td>RW</td><td>R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td></tr></table>

## TIMEOUT_EN

Enables time-out mechanism. When enabled, if SCL stays 0 for too long period of time due to the I2C slave holds SCL at 0 for too long or SCL stucks at 0 due to PCB issue, the master shall terminate the transaction, stop the internal state machine and assert the time-out interrupt. The MCU shall handle this case appropriately and reset the master and FIFO address before reissuing the transaction again. If this option is disabled, the hardware timer will not count nor expire forever. 

0 Disable 

1 Enable 

## RESET_BUS_BUSY_EN

Enables that software reset can reset bus_busy signal. When enabled, if the master receives software reset, it will reset the bus_busy signal to 0. When disabled, the software reset will have no impact on the bus_busy signal. Normally, the software reset should not reset the bus_busy signal. Used only for debugging. 

0 Disable 

1 Enable 

TRANSFER_LEN_CHANGE 

Specifies whether or not to change the transfer length after the fist transfer is completed. If enabled, the transfers after the first transfer will use the transfer_len_aux parameter. 

## ACKERR_DET_EN

Enables slave ACK error detection. When enabled, if the slave ACK error is detected, the master shall terminate the transaction by issuing a STOP condition and asserting the ACKERR interrupt. The MCU shall handle this case appropriately and reset the FIFO address before reissuing the transaction. If this option is disabled, the controller will ignore slave ACK error and keep on scheduled transaction. 

## 0 Disable

## 1 Enable

## DIR_CHANGE

Used for combined transfer format, where the direction of transfer is to be changed from write to read after the FIRST RS condition. Note that when set to 1, the transfers after the direction change will be based on the transfer_len_aux parameter. 

0 Disable 

1 Enable 

## CLK_EXT_EN

I2C spec allows slaves to hold the SCL line low if it is not yet ready for further processing. Therefore, if this bit is set to 1, the master controller will enter a high wait state until the slave releases the SCL line. 

## RS_STOP

In the LS/FS mode, this bit affects the multi-transfer transaction only. It controls whether or not the REPEATED-START condition is used between transfers. The last ending transfer always ends with a STOP. In the HS mode, this bit must be set to 1. 

0 Use STOP 

Use REPEATED-START

<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td colspan="16">TRANSFER_LEN</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h1</td></tr></table>

TRANSFER_LEN[15:0] 

Indicates the number of data bytes to be transferred in 1 transfer unit (excluding slave address byte). Note: The value must be set to bigger than 1; otherwise no transfer will take place. 


I2CREG+006 Transfer length register (number of bytes per TRANSFER_LEN_ Ch transfer) AUX


<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td colspan="16">TRANSFER_LEN_AUX</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h0</td><td>‘h1</td></tr></table>


TRANSFER_LEN_AUX[15:0] 


This field is valid only when dir_change or transfer_len_change is set to 1. This indicates the number of data bytes to be transferred in 1 transfer unit (excluding slave address byte) for the transfers following the direction change or transfer_len_change, i.e. if dir_change = 1, the first write transfer length will depend on transfer_len, while the second read transfer length will depend on transfer_len_aux. Dir change is always after the first transfer. Similarly, the transfer length change is always after the first transfer. Note: The value must be set to bigger than 1; otherwise no transfer will take place. 


I2CREG+001 Transaction length register (number of transfers TRANSAC_LE 8h per transaction) N


<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="8">TRANSAC_LEN</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>&#x27;h1</td></tr></table>


TRANSAC_LEN[7:0] 


Indicates the number of transfers to be transferred in 1 transaction. Note: The value must be set to bigger than 1; otherwise no transfer will take place.

Sets up the wait delay between consecutive transfers when the RS_STOP bit is set to 0. The unit is same as the half pulse width.

TIMING 

<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td>DATA REA D ADJ</td><td colspan="3">DATA_READ_TIME</td><td></td><td colspan="3">SAMPLE_CNT_DIV</td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="6">STEP_CNT_DIV</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td>R/W</td><td colspan="3">R/W</td><td></td><td colspan="3">R/W</td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="6">R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td>&#x27;h0</td><td colspan="3">&#x27;h1</td><td></td><td colspan="3">&#x27;h3</td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="6">&#x27;h3</td></tr></table>

LS/FS only. This register is used to control the output waveform timing. Each half pulse width (i,e. each high or low pulse) is equal to (step_cnt_div+1)*[(sample_cnt_div+1)*1/13MHz] 

SAMPLE_CNT_DIV[2:0] Used for LS/FS only. This adjusts the width of each sample. Sample width = (sample_cnt_div+1)*1/13MHz. Minimum value: 0. 

STEP_CNT_DIV[5:0] Specifies the number of samples per half pulse width (i.e. each high or low pulse). Minimum value: 1. 

## DATA_READ_ADJ

When set to 1, the data latch in sampling time during master reads are adjusted according to the DATA_READ_TIME value. Otherwise, by default, the data are latched in at half of the high pulse width point. 

DATA_READ_TIME[2:0] 

This value is valid only when DATA_READ_ADJ is set to 1. Can be used to adjust so that the data are latched in at earlier sampling points (assuming the data are settled by then). This value must be set to smaller than or equal to half the high pulse width.

High-speed mode register 

HS 

<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td></td><td colspan="3">HS_SAMPLE_CNT DIV</td><td></td><td colspan="3">HS_STEP_CNT_DIV</td><td></td><td colspan="3">MASTER_CODE</td><td></td><td></td><td>HS_NACKERR_DET_EN</td><td>HS_EN</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td></td><td colspan="3">R/W</td><td></td><td colspan="3">R/W</td><td></td><td colspan="3">R/W</td><td></td><td></td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td></td><td colspan="3">0</td><td></td><td colspan="3">1</td><td></td><td colspan="3">0</td><td></td><td></td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr></table>

This register contains options to support high-speed operation features. Each HS half pulse width (i.e. each high or low pulse) = (hs_step_cnt_div+1)*[(hs_sample_cnt_div+1)*1/13MHz] 

HS_SAMPLE_CNT_DIV[2:0] 

When the high-speed mode is entered after the master code transfer is completed, the sample width will become dependent on this parameter. Minimum value: 0 

## HS_STEP_CNT_DIV[2:0]

When the high-speed mode is entered after the master code transfer is completed, the number of samples per half pulse width will become dependent on this value. Minimum value: 

MASTER_CODE[2:0] 

The 3-bit programmable value for the master code to be transmitted. 

HS_NACKERR_DET_EN 

Enables NACKERR detection during the master code transmission. When enabled, if NACK is not received after the master code is transmitted, the transaction will be terminated with a STOP condition. 

HS_EN Enables the high-speed transaction. Note: rs_stop must be set to 1 as well.

SOFT_RESET 

When written with 1’b1, one pulse soft reset is used as synchronous reset to reset the I2C internal hardware circuits.

BUS_BUSY 

For debugging only. Valid when bus_detect_en is 1. bus_busy = 1 indicates a start transaction has been detected and no stop condition has been detected yet. 

MASTER_WRITE For debugging only. 1 = Current transfer is in the master write dir 

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-20/a0b5e3cf-5739-46d1-96ae-050c03350f29/0c52529ffe6af17edb6e53ca772905481d5e02650cb429a9d7a9cb9730026def.jpg)


MASTER_READ 

For debugging only. 1 = Current transfer is in the master read dir 

## MASTER_STATE[4:0]

For debugging only. Reads back the current master_state. 

0 Idle state 

1 I2C master is preparing for sending out the start bit, SCL = 1, SDA = 1 

2 I2C master is sending out the start bit, SCL = 1, SDA = 0 

I2C master/slave is preparing for transmitting data bit, SCL = 0, SDA = data bit (data bit can be changed when SCL = 0) 

I2C master/slave is transmitting data bit, SCL = 1, SDA = data bit (data bit is stable when SCL = 1) 

5 I2C master/slave is preparing for transmitting the ACK bit, SCL = 0, SDA = ack (the ACK bit can be changed when SCL = 0) 

I2C master/slave is transmitting the ACK bit, SCL = 1, SDA = 0 (the ACK bit is stable when SCL = 1) 

7 I2C master is preparing for sending out stop bit or repeated-start bit, SCL = 0, SDA = 0/1 (0 means stop bit; 1 means repeated-start bit) 

I2C master is sending out stop bit or repeated-start bit, SCL = 1, SDA = 1/0 (1 means stop bit; 0 means repeated-start bit) 

9 I2C master is in delay start between two transfers, SCL = 1, SDA = 1 

10 I2C master is in FIFO wait state. For writing transaction, it means FIFO is empty and I2C master is waiting for DMA controller to write data into FIFO. For reading transaction, it means FIFO is full and I2C master is waiting for DMA controller to read data from FIFO, SCL = 0, SDA = don’t care; 

12 I2C master is preparing for sending out data bit of master code. This state is used only in high-speed transaction, SCL = 0, SDA = data bit of master code (data bit of master code can be changed when SCL = 0) 

C master is sending out data bit of master code. This state is used only in high-speed transaction, SCL = 1, SDA = data bit of master code (data bit of master code is stable when SCL = 1) 

14 I2C master/slave is preparing for transmitting the NACK bit, SCL = 0, SDA = NACK bit (the NACK bit can be changed when SCL = 0). This state is used only in high-speed transaction. 

15 I2C master/slave is transmitting the NACK bit, SCL = 1, SDA = 1. This state is used only in high-speed transaction.

VERSION_CODE[7:0] Hardware version code 

4.9 

## Real Time Clock

## 4.9.1 General Description

The Real-Time Clock (RTC) module provides time and data information, as well as 32.768 kHz clock. By configuring pin XOSC32_ENB, the use of the 32k crystal can be determined, i.e. to use a 32k crystal or not. The RTC block has an independent power supply. When the mobile handset is powered off, a dedicated regulator supplies the RTC block. If the main battery is not present, a backup supply such as a small mercury cell battery or a large capacitor is used. In addition to providing timing data, an alarm interrupt is generated and can be used to power up the baseband core. Regulator interrupts corresponding to seconds, minutes, hours and days can be generated whenever the time counter value reaches a maximum value (e.g. 59 for seconds and minutes, 23 for hours, etc.). The year span is supported up to 2,127. The maximum day-of-month values, which depend on the leap year condition, are stored in the RTC block.

In addition, it is high after the reset from low to high due to RTC reload process.</td></tr><tr><td>5</td><td>RELOAD</td><td>RELOAD</td><td>Reloads the values from RTC domain to core domainGenerally the RTC will reload and synchronize the data from RTC to core when being reset from 0 to 1. <table><tr><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>I</td><td>I</td><td>I</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>RW</td><td>RW</td><td>RW</td><td>RW</td><td>RW</td><td>RW</td><td>RW</td><td>RW</td><td>RW</td><td>RW</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td></tr></table>

Overview This register activates or de-activates the IRQ generation when the TC counter reaches its maximum value. 

<table><tr><td>Bit (s)</td><td>Mnemonic</td><td>Name</td><td>Description</td></tr><tr><td>9</td><td>1/8SECCII</td><td>SECCII_1_8</td><td>Sets the bit to 1 to activate the IRQ at each 1/8 of a second update</td></tr><tr><td>8</td><td>1/4SECCII</td><td>SECCII_1_4</td><td>Sets the bit to 1 to activate the IRQ at each 1/4 of a second update</td></tr><tr><td>7</td><td>1/2SECCII</td><td>SECCII_1_2</td><td>Sets the bit to 1 to activate the IRQ at each 1/2 of a second update</td></tr><tr><td>6</td><td>YEACII</td><td>YEACII</td><td>Sets the bit to 1 to activate the IRQ at each year update</td></tr><tr><td>5</td><td>MTHCII</td><td>MTHCII</td><td>Sets the bit to 1 to activate the IRQ at each month update</td></tr><tr><td>4</td><td>DOWCII</td><td>DOWCII</td><td>Sets the bit to 1 to activate the IRQ at each day-of-week update</td></tr><tr><td>3</td><td>DOMCII</td><td>DOMCII</td><td>Sets the bit to 1 to activate the IRQ at each day-of-month update</td></tr><tr><td>2</td><td>HOUCII</td><td>HOUCII</td><td>Sets the bit to 1 to activate the IRQ at each hour update</td></tr><tr><td>1</td><td>MINCII</td><td>MINCII</td><td>Sets the bit to 1 to activate the IRQ at each minute update</td></tr><tr><td>0</td><td>SECCII</td><td>SECCII</td><td>Sets this bit to 1 to activate the IRQ at each second update</td></tr></table>


A0710010 RTC_AL_MASK RTC alarm mask


<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Mne</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>YEA_MSK</td><td>MTH_MSK</td><td>DOW_MSK</td><td>DOM_MSK</td><td>HOU_MSK</td><td>MIN_MSK</td><td>SEC_MSK</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>RW</td><td>RW</td><td>RW</td><td>RW</td><td>RW</td><td>RW</td><td>RW</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td></tr></table>

verview The alarm condition for alarm IRQ generation depends on whether or not the corresponding bit in this register is masked. Note that if all bits 1 in RTC_AL_MASK are set (i.e. RTC_AL_MASK= 0x7f) and PWREN = 1 in RTC_BBPU, it means the alarm will come every second, not disabled. 

<table><tr><td>Bit (s)</td><td>Mnemonic</td><td>Name</td><td>Description</td></tr><tr><td>6</td><td>YEA_MSK</td><td>YEA_MSK</td><td>0: Condition (RTC_TC_YEA = RTC_AL_YEA) is checked to generate the alarm signal.1: Condition (RTC_TC_YEA = RTC_AL_YEA) is masked, i.e. the value of RTC_TC_YEA does not affect the alarm IRQ generation.</td></tr><tr><td>5</td><td>MTH_MSK</td><td>MTH_MSK</td><td>0: Condition (RTC_TC_MTH = RTC_AL_MTH) is checked to generate the alarm signal.1: Condition (RTC_TC_MTH = RTC_AL_MTH) is masked, i.e. the value of RTC_TC_MTH does not affect the alarm IRQ generation.</td></tr><tr><td>4</td><td>DOW_MSK</td><td>DOW_MSK</td><td>0: Condition (RTC_TC_DOW = RTC_AL_DOW) is checked to generate the alarm signal.1: Condition (RTC_TC_DOW = RTC_AL_DOW) is masked, i.e. the value of RTC_TC_DOW does not affect the alarm IRQ generation.</td></tr><tr><td>3</td><td>DOM_MSK</td><td>DOM_MSK</td><td>0: Condition (RTC_TC_DOM = RTC_AL_DOM) is checked to generate the alarm signal.1: Condition (RTC_TC_DOM = RTC_AL_DOM) is masked, i.e. the value of RTC_TC_DOM does not affect the alarm IRQ generation.</td></tr><tr><td>2</td><td>HOU_MSK</td><td>HOU_MSK</td><td>0: Condition (RTC_TC_HOU = RTC_AL_HOU) is checked to generate the alarm signal.1: Condition (RTC_TC_HOU = RTC_AL_HOU) is masked, i.e. the value of RTC_TC_HOU does not affect the alarm IRQ generation.</td></tr><tr><td>1</td><td>MIN_MSK</td><td>MIN_MSK</td><td>0: Condition (RTC_TC_MIN = RTC_AL_MIN) is checked to generate the alarm signal.1: Condition (RTC_TC_MIN = RTC_AL_MIN) is masked, i.e. the value of RTC_TC_MIN does not affect the alarm IRQ generation.</td></tr><tr><td>0</td><td>SEC_MSK</td><td>SEC_MSK</td><td>0: Condition (RTC_TC_SEC = RTC_AL_SEC) is checked to generate the alarm signal.1: Condition (RTC_TC_SEC = RTC_AL_SEC) is masked, i.e. the value of RTC_TC_SEC does not affect the alarm IRQ generation.</td></tr></table>

A071002C RTC_TC_YEA RTC year time counter register


<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Mne</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="7">TC_YEAR</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="7">RW</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td></tr></table>

<table><tr><td>Bit (s)</td><td>Mnemonic</td><td>Name</td><td>Description</td></tr><tr><td>6:0</td><td>TC_YEAR</td><td>TC_YEAR</td><td>Year initial value for the time counter. Range: 0 ~ 127 (2000-2127).The software can bias the year as multiples of 4 for the internal leap-year formula. Here are 3 examples: 2000 ~ 2127, 1972 ~ 2099 and 1904 ~ 2031. To simplify the process, the RTC hardware treats all 4-multiple as leap years. If the range you define includes non-leap 4-multiple year (e.g. 2100), please adjust it to the correct date by yourselves. (e.g. change Feb. <eq>29^{th}</eq>, 2100 to Mar. <eq>1^{st}</eq>, 2100).It is suggested to bias the range to be bigger than 1900 and smaller than 2100 to evade the manual adjustment, i.e. the bias values are suggested to be in the range of [-28,-96], that are (1972~ 2099) ~ (1904~ 2031).The formal leap formula:If year modulo 400 is 0 then leapElse if year modulo 100 is 0 then no_leapElse if year modulo 4 is 0 then leapElse no_leap</td></tr></table>

## Overview The function is only active when RTC_POWERKEY1 and RTC_POWERKEY2 match the correct values.

<table><tr><td>Bit (s)</td><td>Mnemonic</td><td>Name</td><td>Description</td></tr><tr><td>15</td><td>EMBCK_SEL_OPTION</td><td>EMBCK_SEL_OPTION</td><td>Reserved bitPlease keep it to 0x0.</td></tr><tr><td>14:11</td><td>EOSC_RSV</td><td>EOSC_RSV</td><td>EOSC32 reserved bitsPlease keep it to 0x0.</td></tr><tr><td>10</td><td>EOSC_CHOP_EN</td><td>EOSC_CHOP_EN</td><td>Enables EOSC32 chopper0: Disable1: EnablePlease keep it to 0x1.</td></tr><tr><td>9:8</td><td>EOSC_OPT</td><td>EOSC_OPT</td><td>EOSC32 optional bitsPlease keep it to 0x0.</td></tr><tr><td>7:6</td><td>EMB_MODE</td><td>EMBCK_SEL</td><td>Mode setting for crystal removal casePlease keep it to 0x0.</td></tr><tr><td>5</td><td>XOSC32_ENB</td><td>XOSC32_ENB</td><td>XOSC32_ENBCan read pin XOSC32_ENB configuration to know the 32k crystal usage.0: Use 32k crystal.1: Does not use 32k crystal.</td></tr><tr><td>4:0</td><td>XOSCCALI</td><td>XOSCCALI</td><td>Controls XOSC32/EOSC32 cali.If XOSC32_ENB(RTC_OSC32CON[5]) = 0, XOSCCALI will control the bias current for 32k xtal in XOSC32.When powerkeys do not match, the default value will be 0xf.If XOSC32_ENB(RTC_OSC32CON[5]) = 1, XOSCCALI will be the trimming value for EOSC32. The software needs to find the best trimming value for EOSC32 when it is the first power-on by frequency meter. When powerkeys do not match, the default value will be 0xf.</td></tr></table>

OSC32CON is not protected by RTC_PROT because RTC_PROT needs 32.768 kHz clock to unlock. Similarly, to modify RTC_OSC32CON, writing RTC_WRTGR is not needed, neither. To protect the OSC32 control bits, follow the update sequence to update RTC_OSC32CON. After the updating sequence is completed, reload to acquire the internal RTC_OSC32CON. This register needs to be initialized before writing powerkeys match. 

## Update sequence:

Step 1: Write RTC_OSC32CON = 0x1a57 and wait until CBUSY = 0 

Step 2: Write RTC_OSC32CON = 0x2b68 and wait until CBUSY = 0 

Step 3: Write the real value you would like to write RTC_OSC32CON and wait until CBUSY = 0 

Step 4: Return to step 1 if you need to modify RTC_OSC32CON again. 

Note: RTC_OSC32CON should be set before writing correct values to POWERKEY1 and POWERKEY2. 


A0710050 RTC_POWERKEY1 RTC_POWERKEY1 register

These register sets are used to determine if the real time clock has been programmed by the software, i.e. the time value in real time clock is correct. When the real-time clock is first powered on, the register contents are all undefined, and therefore the time values shown are incorrect. The software needs to know if the real-time clock has been programmed. Hence, the two registers are defined to solve this power-on issue. After the software programs the correct value, the two register sets will not need to be updated. In addition to programming the correct time value, when the contents of the register sets are wrong, the interrupt will not be generated. Therefore, the real-time clock will not generate the interrupts before the software programs the registers, and unwanted interrupt due to wrong time value will not occur. The correct values of these two register sets are: 

RTC_POWERKEY1 A357h RTC_POWERKEY2 67D2h 

0000

It takes effect once and returns to zero when done.In some cases, RTC is faster or slower than the standard. To change RTC_TC_SEC from being coarse may cause alarm problems. RTC_DIFF provides a finer time unit. An internal 15-bit counter accumulates in each 32,768-Hz clock. Entering a non-zero value to RTC_DIFF will cause the internal RTC counter to increase or decrease RTC_DIFF when RTC_DIFF changes to zero again. RTC_DIFF represents 2&#x27;s complement.For example, if you fill 0xff into RTC_DIFF, the internal counter will decrease by 1 when RTC_DIFF returns to 0. In other words, you can only use RTC_DIFF continuously if RTC_DIFF is equal to 0 now.Note: RTC_DIFF ranges from 0x800 (-2048) to 0x7fd (2045). 0x7ff &amp; 0x7fe are forbidden.</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td></tr></table>

<table><tr><td>Bit (s)</td><td>Mnemonic</td><td>Name</td><td>Description</td></tr><tr><td>0</td><td>WRTGR</td><td>WRTGR</td><td>Enables the transfers from core to RTCAfter you modify all the RTC registers and would like to change, write 1 to RTC_WRTGR to trigger the transfer. The prior writing operation is queued in the core power domain. The pending data will not be transferred to the RTC domain until WRTGR = 1.After WRTGR = 1, the pending data will be transferred to the RTC domain sequentially in order of register addresses, from low to high. For example: RTC_BBPU -&gt;RTC_IRQ_EN -&gt;RTC_CII_EN -&gt;RTC_AL_MASK -&gt;RTC_TC_SEC -&gt;etc. CBUSY in RTC_BBPU is equal to 1 in the writing process. Observe CBUSY to determine when the transmission is completed.</td></tr></table>

SR=1.0: RTC_GPIO higher slew output.1: RTC_GPIO slower slew output.</td></tr><tr><td>3</td><td>LPRST</td><td>LPRST</td><td>Resets LPDETBThis only takes effect when LPEN = 1.</td></tr><tr><td>2</td><td>LPEN</td><td>LPEN</td><td>Enables LPDETBLP initialization sequence:Write LPEN = 1, LPRST = 0. Write RTC_WRTGR = 1. wait cbusy down.Write LPEN = 1, LPRST = 1. Write RTC_WRTGR = 1. wait cbusy down.Write LPEN = 1, LPRST = 0. Write RTC_WRTGR = 1. wait cbusy down.</td></tr><tr><td>0</td><td>VBAT_LPSTA_RAW</td><td>VBAT_LPSTA_RAW</td><td>Indicates the battery has been in LP stateThe software needs to clear this bit for the next time use0: VBAT has not been in LP state.VBAT has been in LP state.Note: VBAT LP state = VBAT &lt; 2.5V</td></tr></table>

If Vcore always exists, the software can trust the registers and wait for the LP interrupt from RTC if the 32.768 kHz clock stops or has been stopped. 

However, nothing can be trusted after the battery is off (Vrtc may drop). In every booting time, the software has to check if LPSTA_RAW = 1. (LP_STA = LPSTA_RAW & LP_IRQ_EN) If true, then the RTC contents will no longer be trusted just like powerkeys do not match. You have to initialize the RTC contents in this case. 

## 4.10 Auxiliary ADC Unit

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-20/a0b5e3cf-5739-46d1-96ae-050c03350f29/c25294125169a79b075350f1817150140f2c796a67680249f5fa053e73928111.jpg)


The auxiliary ADC unit is used to monitor the status of the battery and charger, to identify the plugged peripheral, and to perform temperature measurement. There are 7 input channels allowing diverse applications in this unit. 

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-20/a0b5e3cf-5739-46d1-96ae-050c03350f29/2b15ec85e67616087e7b049dc32305efe177079788de4cde936f753958989443.jpg)



Figure 42. Auxadc Architecture


Each channel operates in one of the two modes: immediate mode or timer-triggered mode. The mode of each channel can be individually selected through register AUXADC_CON0. For example, if the flag SYN0 in register AUXADC_CON0 is set, channel 0 will be set in the timer-triggered mode. Otherwise, the channel will operate in the immediate mode. 

In the immediate mode, the A/D converter samples the value once only when the flag in the AUXADC_CON1 register is set. For example, if the flag IMM0 in AUXADC_CON1 is set, the A/D converter will sample the data for channel 0. The IMM flags must be cleared and set again to initialize another sampling. 

The value sampled for channel 0 is stored in register AUXADC_DAT0, and the value for channel 1 is stored in register AUXADC_DAT1, and so on. 

If the AUTOSET flag in register AUXADC_CON3 is set, the auto-sample function will be enabled. The A/D converter samples the data for the channel in which the corresponding data register is read. For example, in the case where the SYN1 flag is not set, the AUTOSET flag is set. When the data register AUXADC_DAT0 is read, the A/D converter will sample the next value for channel 1 immediately. 

If multiple channels are selected at the same time, the task will be performed sequentially on every selected channel. For example, if AUXADC_CON1 is set to 0x3f, i.e. 6 channels are selected, the state machine in the unit will start sampling from channel 6 to channel 0 and save the values of each input channel in respective registers. The same process also applies to the timer-triggered mode. 

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-20/a0b5e3cf-5739-46d1-96ae-050c03350f29/5b3fcdfb0fe4bcbf8664281b9b78da99f18f24517d1deac9fded458a9b5acedb.jpg)


In the timer-triggered mode, the A/D converter samples the value for the channels in which the corresponding SYN flags are set when the TDMA timer counts to the value specified in register TDMA_AUXEV1 placed in the TDMA timer. For example, if AUXADC_CON0 is set to 0x3f, the 6 channels will be selected to be in the timer-triggered mode. The state machine will sample the 6 channels sequentially and save the values in registers from AUXADC_DAT0 to AUXADC_DAT5, as it does in the immediate mode. 

There is a dedicated timer-triggered scheme for channel 0. The scheme is enabled by setting up the SYN11 flag in register AUXADC_CON2. The timing offset for this event is stored in register TDMA_AUXEV0 in the TDMA timer. The sampled data triggered by this specific event are stored in register AUXADC_DAT11. It is used to separate the results of two individual software routines that perform actions on the auxiliary ADC unit. 

AUTOCLRn in register AUXADC_CON3 is set when it is intended to sample only once after setting up the timer-triggered mode. If the AUTOCLR1 flag is set, after the data for the channels in the timer-triggered mode are stored, the SYNn flags in register AUXADC_CON0 will be cleared. If the AUTOCLR0 flag is set, after the data for channel 0 are stored in register AUXADC_DAT11, the SYN11 flag in register AUXADC_CON2 will be cleared. 

The uses of the immediate mode and timer-triggered mode are mutually exclusive in terms of individual channels. 

There are only two external pins (channel 4 ~ 5) for voltage detection. Other channels (0 ~ 3) are for battery voltage, battery current, charger and battery temperature respectively. Channel 9 is used for audio. 

## Touch panel

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-20/a0b5e3cf-5739-46d1-96ae-050c03350f29/6a7fd2216348b9363f7cfca082d0086365868a5986cf2eb404395b94caaadc4c.jpg)



Figure 43. Touch Panel Circuit Structure


Besides the normal sampling of external input voltage, the AUXADC includes the sampling of the touch panel function. For specified axis, the software should program AUX_TS_CMD first then trigger the sample of touch panel in register AUX_TS_CON. The touch panel sampling waveform is shown as the following. After the software polls the status bit in register AUXADC_CON3 to know that the touch panel sample is finished, the software can read back the specified axis value from register AUX_TS_DAT0. 

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-20/a0b5e3cf-5739-46d1-96ae-050c03350f29/6386d4e39998f8fa596aff1e359b71c5fc258553b7f28f04e4fb9c4ddd1009b6.jpg)



Figure 44. Touch Panel Sampling Waveform

A2 ~ A0: Addressing bits 

Mode: 10-bit or 8-bit 

SE/DF: Single end or differential mode 

PD1 ~ 0: Power down command

## AUTOSET

Defines the auto-sample mode of the module. In auto-sample mode, each channel with its sample register being read can start sampling immediately without configuring the control register AUXADC_CON1 again. 

## PUWAIT_EN

Enables the power warm-up period to ensure power stability before the SAR process takes place. It is recommended to activate this field. 

0 The mode is not enabled. 

1 The mode is enabled. 

## SOFT_RST

Software reset AUXADC state machine 

0 Normal function 

1 Reset AUXADC state machine 

## AUTOCLR1

Defines the auto-clear mode of the module for event 1. In the auto-clear mode, each timer-triggered channel acquires samples of specified channels once the 

SYNn bit in register AUXADC_CON0 is set. The SYNn bits are automatically cleared and the channel is not enabled again by the timer event except when the SYNn flags are set again. 

0 The automatic clear mode is not enabled. 

1 The automatic clear mode is enabled. 

## AUTOCLR0

Defines the auto-clear mode of the module for event 0. In the auto-clear mode, the timer-triggered channel 0 acquires the sample once the SYN11 bit in register AUXADC_CON2 is set. The SYN11 bit is automatically cleared and the channel is not enabled again by the timer event 0 except when the SYN11 flag is set again. 

0 The automatic clear mode is not enabled. 

1 The automatic clear mode is enabled. 

## STA

Defines the state of the module 

0 This module is idle. 

1 This module is busy.

## 4.11 USB Device Controller

## 4.11.1 General Description

This chip provides a USB function interface which complies with Universal Serial Bus Specification Rev 1.1. The USB device controller supports only full-speed (12Mbps) operation. The chip can make use of this widely available USB interface to transmit/receive data with USB hosts, typically PC/laptop. 

There are 6 endpoints in the USB device controller besides the mandatory control endpoint, 4 of which are for IN transactions and 2 are for OUT transactions. Word, half-word, and byte access are all allowed for loading and unloading the FIFO. The controller features 4 DMA channels to accelerate the data transfer for ACL and SCO data streams. The features of the endpoints are: 

Endpoint 0: The control endpoint feature 64 bytes FIFO and accommodates maximum packet size of up to 64 bytes. DMA transfer is not supported. 

IN endpoint 1: It features 64 bytes FIFO and accommodates maximum packet size of up to 64 bytes. GDMA Channel 4 Write Transfer is supported. 

IN endpoint 2: It features 64 bytes FIFO and accommodates maximum packet size of up to 64 bytes. GDMA Channel 6 Write Transfer is supported. 

IN endpoint 3: It features 16-byte FIFO and accommodates maximum packet size of 16 bytes. GDMA Transfer is not supported. 

IN endpoint 4: It features 16-byte FIFO and accommodates maximum packet size of 16 bytes. GDMA Transfer is not supported. 

OUT endpoint 1: It features 64 bytes FIFO and accommodates maximum packet size of 64 bytes. GDMA Channel 5 Read Transfer is supported. 

OUT endpoint 2: It features 64 bytes FIFO and accommodates maximum packet size of 64 bytes. GDMA Channel 7 Read Transfer is supported. 

For each endpoint except for the control endpoint, when the packet size is smaller than half the size of the FIFO, at most 2 packets can be buffered. 

This unit is highly software configurable. All endpoints except for the control endpoint can be configured to be a bulk, interrupt or isochronous endpoints. Composite devices are also supported. IN endpoint 1 and OUT endpoint 1 share the same endpoint number, but they can be used separately. So is the situation for the endpoint 2. 

The USB device uses the cable-powered feature for the transceiver but only drains little current. An internal pull-up resistor is integrated across Vbus and D+ signal. The switch on/off of the pull-up resistor can be configured through the internal register. Two additional external serial resistors might be needed to place on the output of D+ and D- signals to make the output impedance equivalent to 28 ~ 44Ohm. 

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-20/a0b5e3cf-5739-46d1-96ae-050c03350f29/950d1a7d0b626de5c4b74aaf0da0ce7fa1041d1d9019f9db1ee3cc12f3aa12d9.jpg)



Figure 45. USB11 controller system diagram



4.11.2 Register Definition



USB+0000h USB function address register



USB_FADDR

When set by the MCU, the core will wait for an SOF token from the time INPKTRDY is set before sending the packet. 

USB_INTROUT

## SWRSTENAB

Set by the MCU to enable the mode in which the device can only be reset by the delayed by other high-priority processes and cannot make it to read the command from the buffer before the hardware resets the device after the reset signal is detected on the bus, the command will be lost. That is why the software-reset mode is effective. When the flag is enabled, the hardware state machine cannot reset itself but by the software. In that sense, the software and hardware can keep synchronous detecting the reset signal. 

RESET The read-only bit is set when Reset signaling is present on the bus. 

RESUME Set by the MCU to generate Resume signaling when the function is in the suspend mode. The MCU should clear this bit after 10ms (maximum 15ms) to end Resume signaling. 

SUSPMODE Set by the USB core when the Suspend mode is entered. Cleared when the Resume bit of this register is set. 

SUSPENAB Set by the MCU to enable device into the Suspend mode when Suspend signaling is received on the bus.

The register determines which endpoint control/status registers are to be accessed at addresses USB+10h to USB+17h. Each IN endpoint and OUT endpoint has its own set of control/status registers. Only one set of IN control/status and one set of OUT control/status registers appear in the memory map at a time. Before accessing the control/status registers of an endpoint, the endpoint number should be written to the USB_INDEX register to ensure that the correct control/status registers appear in the memory map. 

INDEX Index of the endpoint.

USB+0011h USB control/status register for endpoint 0



USB_EP0_CSR


<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td>SSETUPEN D</td><td>SOUTPKTR DY</td><td>SENDSTAL L</td><td>SETUPEND</td><td>DATAEND</td><td>SENTSTAL L</td><td>INPKTRDY</td><td>OUTPKTRD Y</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td>R/WS</td><td>R/WS</td><td>R/WS</td><td>RO</td><td>R/WS</td><td>R/WC</td><td>R/WS</td><td>RO</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td></tr></table>

The register is used for all control/status of endpoint 0. The register is active when the USB_INDEX register is set to 0. 

SSETUPEND The MCU writes 1 to this bit to clear the SETUPEND bit. It is cleared automatically. Only active when a transaction is started. 

SOUTPKTRDY The MCU writes 1 to this bit to clear the OUTPKTRDY bit. It is cleared automatically. Only active when an OUT transaction is started. 

SENDSTALL The MCU writes 1 to this bit to terminate the current transaction. The STALL handshake will be transmitted, and this bit will be cleared automatically. 

SETUPEND This bit will be set when a control transaction ends before the DATAEND bit is set. An interrupt will be generated and FIFO flushed at this time. The bit is cleared by the MCU writing 1 to the SSETUPEND bit. 

DATAEND The MCU sets up this bit: 1. When setting INPKTRDY for the last data packet 2. When clearing OUTPKTRDY after unloading the last data packet 3. When setting INPKTRDY for a zero length data packet It is cleared automatically. 

SENTSTALL This bit is set when a STALL handshake is transmitted. The MCU should clear this bit by writing 0 to it. 

INPKTRDY The MCU sets up this bit after loading a data packet into the FIFO. It is cleared automatically when the data packet is transmitted. An interrupt is generated when this bit is set. 

OUTPKTRDY This bit is set when a data packet is received. An interrupt is generated when this bit is set. The MCU clears this bit by setting up the SOUTPKTRDY bit. 

USB+0016h USB byte count register 

USB_ep0_COUNT
