The register indicates the number of received data bytes in the endpoint 0. The value returned is valid while the OUTPKTRDY bit of the USB_EP0_CSR register is set. The register is active when the USB_INDEX register is set to 0. 

COUNT Number of received data bytes in the endpoint 0 


USB+0010h USB maximum packet size register for IN endpoint 1~4 USB_EP_INMAXP


<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td colspan="8">MAXP</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td colspan="8">R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td colspan="8">0</td></tr></table>

The register holds the maximum packet size for transactions through the currently selected IN endpoint – in units of one byte. When setting up the value, the programmer should note the constraints placed by the USB Specification on the packet size for bulk interrupt and isochronous transactions in full-speed operation. There is an INMAXP register for each IN endpoint except for endpoint 0. The registers are active when the USB_INDEX register is set to 1, 2, 3, and 4 respectively. 

The value written to this register should match the wMaxPacketSize field of the standard endpoint descriptor for the associated endpoint. A mismatch may cause unexpected results. If a value is bigger than the configured IN FIFO size for the endpoint written to the register, the value will be automatically changed to the IN FIFO size. If the value written to the register is smaller than or equal to half the IN FIFO size, two IN packets can be buffered. The configured IN FIFO size for the endpoint 1, 2, and 3 are 16 bytes, 64 bytes and 64 bytes respectively. 

The register is reset to 0. If the register is changed after the packets are sent from the endpoint, the endpoint IN FIFO should be completely flushed after writing the new value to the register. 

MAXP Maximum packet size (unit: one byte) 


USB control/status register #1 for IN endpoint 1~4


<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td>ABORTPKT_EN</td><td>CLRDATATOG</td><td>SENTSTALL</td><td>SENDSTALL</td><td>FLUSHFIFO</td><td>UNDERRUN</td><td>FIFONOTE MPTY</td><td>INPKTRDY</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td>R/W</td><td>WO</td><td>R/WC</td><td>R/W</td><td>WO</td><td>R/WC</td><td>RO</td><td>R/WS</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td></tr></table>

The register provides control and status bits for IN transactions through the currently selected endpoint. There is an INCSR1 register for each IN endpoint except endpoint 0. The registers are active when USB_INDEX register is set to 1, 2, and 3, respectively. 

ABORTPKT_EN 

When MCU writes 1 to ABORTPKT_EN, FLUSHFIFO switches to abort the packet function. This bit should be enabled before FLUSHFIFO is set. If FLUSHFIFO is set and ABORTPKT_EN is enabled, the data loaded into FIFO will be discarded. After the packet is aborted, EP will issue an interrupt. The programmer should wait for this interrupt to make sure the packet is aborted. 

CLRDATATOG 

The MCU writes 1 to this bit to reset the endpoint IN data toggle to 0. 

## SENTSTALL

The bit is set when a STALL handshake is transmitted. The FIFO is flushed and the INPKTRDY bit is cleared. The MCU should clear this bit by writing 0 to this bit. 

SENDSTALL The MCU writes 1 to this bit to issue a STALL handshake to an IN token. The MCU clears this bit to terminate the stall condition. 

FLUSHFIFO The MCU writes 1 to this bit to flush the next packet to be transmitted from the endpoint IN FIFO. The FIFO pointer is reset and the INPKTRDY bit is cleared. If the FIFO contains two packets, FLUSHFIFO will need to be set twice to completely clear the FIFO. FLUSHFIFO should only be used when INPKTRDY is set. At other times, it may cause data corruption. If ABORTPKT_EN is enabled and this bit is set , the function of this bit will become ABORTPKT to abort the next packet to be transmitted from the endpoint IN FIFO and does not need to set INPKTRDY. It is the same with the FLUSHFIFO function. This bit is only active when the endpoint is idle. 

UNDERRUN In isochronous mode, this bit is set when a zero length data packet is sent after receiving an IN token with the INPKTRDY bit not set. In Bulk/Interrupt mode, this bit is set when a NAK is returned in response to an IN token. The MCU should clear this bit by writing a 0 to this bit. 

## FIFONOTEMPTY INPKTRDY

This bit is set when there is at least 1 packet in the IN FIFO. The MCU sets up this bit after loading a data packet into the FIFO. Only active when an IN transaction is started. It is cleared automatically when a data packet is transmitted. An interrupt will be generated (if enabled) when the bit is cleared. 


USB+0012h USB control/status register #2 for IN endpoint 1~4


<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td>AUTOSET</td><td>ISO</td><td>MODE</td><td>DMAENAB</td><td>RFCDATATOG</td><td></td><td></td><td></td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td></td><td></td><td></td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td></td><td></td><td></td></tr></table>

The register provides further control bits for IN transactions through the currently selected endpoint. There is an INCSR2 register for each IN endpoint except for endpoint 0. The registers are active when the USB_INDEX register is set to 1, 2, and 3 respectively. 

AUTOSET 

If the MCU sets up the bit, INPKTRDY will be automatically set when the data of the maximum packet size (value in INMAXP) is loaded into the IN FIFO. If a packet of smaller than the maximum packet size is loaded, INPKTRDY will have to be set manually. When 2 packets are in the IN FIFO, INPKTRDY will also be automatically set when the first packet is sent if the second packet is the maximum packet size. 

## ISO

The MCU sets up this bit to enable the IN endpoint for isochronous transfer and clears it to enable the IN endpoint for bulk/interrupt transfers. 

MODE The MCU sets up this bit to enable the endpoint direction as IN and clears it to enable the endpoint direction as OUT. It is valid only when the same endpoint FIFO is used for both IN and OUT transactions. 

DMAENAB The MCU sets up this bit to enable the DMA request for the IN endpoint. 

FRCDATATOG The MCU sets up this bit to force the endpoint’s IN data toggle to switch after each data packet is sent regardless of whether an ACK has been received. This can be used by interrupt IN endpoints which are used to communicate rate feedback for isochronous endpoints. 


USB+0013h USB maximum packet size register for OUT endpoint 1~2 USB_EP_OUTMAXP


<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td colspan="8">MAXP</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td colspan="8">R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td colspan="8">0</td></tr></table>

This register holds the maximum packet size for transactions through the currently selected OUT endpoint (unit: one byte). When setting up this value, the programmer should note the constraints placed by the USB specification on packet sizes for bulk, interrupt, and isochronous transactions in full speed operation. There is an OUTMAXP register for each OUT endpoint except for endpoint 0. The registers are active when the USB_INDEX register is set to 1 and 2 respectively. 

The value written to this register should match the wMaxPacketSize field of the standard endpoint descriptor for the associated endpoint. A mismatch may cause unexpected results. The total amount of data represented by the value written to this register must not exceed the FIFO size for the OUT endpoint and should not exceed half the FIFO size if double buffering is required. If a value bigger than the configured OUT FIFO size for the endpoint is written to the register, the value will be automatically changed to the OUT FIFO size. If the value written to the register is smaller than or equal to half the OUT FIFO size, two OUT packets can be buffered. The configured IN FIFO size for the endpoint 1, 2, and 3 are both 16, 64, and 64 bytes respectively. 

MAXP Maximum packet size (unit: one byte) 


USB+0014h



USB control/status register #1 for OUT endpoint 1~2



USB_EP_OUTCSR


<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td>CLRDATATO G</td><td>SENTSTAL L</td><td>SENDSTAL L</td><td>FLUSHFIF O</td><td>DATAERRO R</td><td>OVERRU N</td><td>FIFOFULL</td><td>OUTPKTRDY</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td>WO</td><td>R/WC</td><td>R/W</td><td>WO</td><td>RO</td><td>R/WC</td><td>RO</td><td>R/WC</td></tr></table>


MediaTek Confidential 


<table><tr><td>Reset</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td></tr></table>

The register provides control status bits for OUT transactions through the currently selected endpoint. The registers are active when the USB_INDEX register is set to 1 and 2 respectively. 

CLRDATATOG The MCU writes 1 to this bit to reset the endpoint data toggle to 0. 

SENTSTALL The bit is set when a STALL handshake is transmitted. The MCU should clear this bit by writing 0 to it. 

SENDSTALL The MCU writes 1 to this bit to issue a STALL handshake. The MCU clears this bit to terminate the stall condition. This bit will have no effect if the OUT endpoint is in the isochronous mode. 

FLUSHFIFO The MCU writes 1 to this bit to flush the next packet to be read from the endpoint OUT FIFO. If the FIFO contains two packets, FLUSHFIFO will need to be set twice to completely clear the FIFO. FLUSHFIFO should only be used when OUTPKTRDY is set. At other times, it may cause data corruption. 

DATAERROR The bit is set when OUTPKTRDY is set if the data packet has a CRC or bit-stuff error. It is cleared when OUTPKTRDY is cleared. This bit is only valid in the isochronous mode. 

OVERRUN The bit will be set if an OUT packet cannot be loaded into the OUT FIFO. The MCU should clear the bit by writing 0 to it. This bit is only valid in the isochronous mode. 

FIFOFULL This bit is set when no more packets can be loaded into the OUT FIFO. 

OUTPKTRDY The bit is set when a data packet has been received. The MCU should clear (write 0 to it) the bit when the packet is unloaded from the OUT FIFO. An interrupt will be generated when the bit is set. When the null packet is received, OUTPKTRDY will be set after USB_INTROUT1 is high. 


USB+0015h



USB control/status register #2 for OUT endpoint 1~2


<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td>AUTOCLEAR</td><td>ISO</td><td>DMAENAB</td><td>DMAMODE</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td></tr></table>

The register provides further control bits for OUT transactions through the currently selected endpoint. The registers are active when the USB_INDEX register is set to 1 and 2 respectively. 

AUTOCLEAR 

If the MCU sets up this bit, the OUTPKTRDY bit will be automatically cleared when a packet of OUTMAXP bytes is unloaded from the OUT FIFO. When packets of 

smaller than the maximum packet size are unloaded, OUTPKTRDY will have to be cleared manually. 

## ISO

The MCU sets up this bit to enable the OUT endpoint for isochronous transfers and clears it to enable the OUT endpoint for bulk/interrupt transfers. 

## DMAENAB

The MCU sets up this bit to enable the DMA request for the OUT endpoint. 

## DMAMODE

Two modes of DMA operation are supported: DMA mode 0 in which a DMA request is generated for all received packets, together with an interrupt (if enabled). DMA mode 1 in which a DMA request (but no interrupt) is generated for OUT packets of size OUTMAXP bytes and an interrupt (but no DMA request) is generated for OUT packets of any other size. The MCU sets up the bit to select DMA mode 1 and clears this bit to select DMA mode 0.

## 4.11.3 System Integration Guide

The target audience of this section is the software engineer. 

The USB device controller features one control endpoint and 6 other endpoints. The configuration of interfaces and endpoints can be accommodated by software for specific functions, basically supporting Bluetooth HCI, and device firmware upgrade. 

Bluetooth HCI transport layer defines the configuration of endpoints and interfaces. 


<sup></sup> One voice channel with 16-bit encoding


<table><tr><td>Endpoint</td><td>Endpoint type</td><td>Max. packet size (bytes)</td><td>Max. bandwidth (bytes/ms)</td><td>Min. bandwidth (bytes/ms)</td><td>Double buffer in controller</td><td>Generic DMA</td></tr><tr><td>Endpoint 0 (command)</td><td>Control</td><td>64</td><td></td><td></td><td>No</td><td>No</td></tr><tr><td>Endpoint 1 IN</td><td>Bulk (IN)</td><td>64</td><td></td><td>1,024</td><td>No</td><td>Yes</td></tr><tr><td>Endpoint 2 OUT</td><td>Bulk (OUT)</td><td>64</td><td></td><td>1,024</td><td>No</td><td>Yes</td></tr><tr><td>Endpoint 2 IN</td><td>Bulk (IN)</td><td>64</td><td>1,024</td><td></td><td>No</td><td>Yes</td></tr><tr><td>Endpoint 2 OUT</td><td>Bulk (OUT)</td><td>64</td><td>1,024</td><td></td><td>No</td><td>Yes</td></tr><tr><td>Endpoint 3 IN</td><td>Interrupt (IN)</td><td>16</td><td></td><td>16</td><td>No</td><td>No</td></tr><tr><td>Endpoint 4 IN</td><td>Interrupt (IN)</td><td>16</td><td></td><td>16</td><td>No</td><td>No</td></tr></table>

When the maximum packet size is smaller than one half of the device FIFO size (64 bytes), the double buffer will be automatically enabled by hardware. 

The pull-up resister on the USB transceiver is initially disconnected when boot-up. No external resistor is required. The software should enable it after performing the configuration of the USB device controller. 

The clock, interrupt and DMA are defined as the system infrastructure. It requires several steps to bring up the USB. Those steps should be done in sequence to prevent from malfunction. 

## Power-on

1. Enable USB PLL. 

2. Enable USB clock after USB PLL is settled. 

3. Unmask the USB interrupt in the interrupt controller. 

4. Enable the pull-up resistor. 

The USB device controller can generate the interrupt when conditions are met as defined in USB_INTRINE, USB_INTROUTE, and USB_INTRUSBE. 

The generic DMA controller is used to move data from or to the USB device controller. The USB device controller will use at most 4 DMA channels for ACL and SCO. The user should use the half-channel DMA since only the half-channel DMA has the hardware flow control. 

The USB FIFO provides byte and word accesses to the read/write port of USB_EP0_FIFO, 

USB_EP1_FIFO, USB_EP2_FIFO, USB_EP3_FIFO and USB_EP4_FIFO. If the data buffer allocated in memory is word aligned, the user can enable word transfer in the DMA controller. If the data buffer allocated in memory is not word aligned, the user should set to byte aligned and set B2W in DMAx_CON to 1 to enable fast byte-to-word transfer. Please refer to the DMA section for more details. 

## Power-on sequence after plug-in

1. Turn on Vusb (PHY 3.3v power) – The control register is in PMIC document. 

2. Turn on USB AHB clock (78MHz) – The control register is in config document. 

3. Turn on internal 48MHz PLL – The control register is in config document. 

4. Wait for 50 usec. (PHY 3.3v power stable time) 

5. Turn on USB PHY BIAS current control  reg[USB+08C1h] bit3 = 1. (RG_USB11_FSLS_ENBGRI). 

6. Wait for 10 usec. 

7. Set up D+ pull up register for connecting Host  reg[USB+0240h] bit 0 =1(PUB) 

## Power-off sequence after plug-out

1. Release D+ pull up register for disconnecting host  Set reg[USB+0240h] bit 0 =0 (PUB) 

2. Turn off USB PHY BIAS current control  reg[USB+08C1h] bit3 = 0. (RG_USB11_FSLS_ENBGRI). 

3. Turn off Vusb (PHY 3.3v power) – The control register is in PMIC document. 

## 4.12 Accessory Detector

## 1.1.1 General Description

The hardware accessory detector (ACCDET) detects plug-in/out of multiple types of external components. Based on the suggested circuit (see Figure 46), this design supports 3 types of external components, which are microphone, hook-switch and TV-OUT line. This design uses the internal 2-bit comparator to separate external components. The de-bounce scheme is also supported to resist uncertain input noises. When the plug-in/out state is stable, the PWM unit of ACCDET will enable the comparator, MBIAS and threshold voltage of the comparator periodically for the plugging detection. With suitable PWM settings, very low-power consumption can be achieved when the detection feature is enabled. In order to compensate the delay between the detection login and comparator, the delay enabling scheme is adopted. Given the suitable delay number compared to the rising edge of PWM high pulse, the stable plugging state can be prorogated to digital detection logic. Then the correct plugging state can be detected and reported. 

Figure 47 shows the state machine without TV-OUT mode. The state machine is executed by the software to control the ACCDET design. The ACCDET design will send one interrupt to acknowledge the software after the ACCDET input state is changed and the duration of the state is longer than de-bounce time. The software needs to read out the memorized ACCDET input state and follow the recommend state machine to program the register in it. 

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-20/a0b5e3cf-5739-46d1-96ae-050c03350f29/410db2edc4c07b830b808053a6a77909742301ec9c6530529a8aa27a6bd9ea2c.jpg)



Figure 46. Suggested accessory detection circuit


![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-20/a0b5e3cf-5739-46d1-96ae-050c03350f29/280317cb764d3c427d4297a087257a5af42962fecf6e476f248c47908b184957.jpg)



Figure 47. State machine between microphone and hook-switch plug-in/out change


## 1.1.2 Pulse Width Modulation

The ACCDET design also provides one Pulse-Width-Modulation (PWM) to enable the comparator, microphone’s bias current and the threshold voltage of the comparator periodically. With suitable PWM and settings for delayed enabling, the ACCDET can achieve very low power consumption and accurate plug-in/out detection. Figure 48 shows a timing diagram example of such PWM design. The output from PWM keeps being at “0” until the value of the counter is smaller than the programmed threshold. 

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-20/a0b5e3cf-5739-46d1-96ae-050c03350f29/bf3e65c4e6eb6f9efa26f9303c9bd83553743030dc05442ac84e37f7129afe3a.jpg)



Figure 48. PWM waveform

Overview This register defines the waiting period before plug-in/out or release events are considered stable. If the de-bounce setting is too small, the plug-in/out will be too sensitive and detect too many unexpected plug-ins/outs. The suitable de-bounce time setting must be adjusted for the user's demand.

<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>31</td><td>30</td><td>29</td><td>28</td><td>27</td><td>26</td><td>25</td><td>24</td><td>23</td><td>22</td><td>21</td><td>20</td><td>19</td><td>18</td><td>17</td><td>16</td></tr><tr><td>Mne</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td></tr><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Mne</td><td colspan="16">DEBOUNCE3</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td colspan="16">RW</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>1</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td></tr></table>

Overview This register defines the waiting period before plug-in/out or release events are considered stable. If the de-bounce setting is too small, the plug-in/out will be too sensitive and detect too many unexpected plug-ins/outs. The suitable de-bounce time setting must be adjusted for the user's demand.

Overview 

When the interrupt of ACCDET is asserted, IRQ_CLR must be set to 1 to clear the interrupt status. This bit will pause all activities in the ACCDET design until both interrupt status and IRQ_CLR are cleared. The software should write 1 to IRQ_CLR first to clear the interrupt (IRQ). After that, if pclk gating is enabled, the software should read 

ACCDET_IRQ_STS again to make IRQ_CLR self-reset to 0.

## 4.13 SD Memory Card Controller

## 4.13.1 Introduction

The controller fully supports the SD memory card bus protocol as defined in SD Memory Card Specification Part 1 Physical Layer Specification version 2.0. 

Furthermore, the controller also partially supports the SDIO card specification version 2.0. However, the controller can only be configured as the host of the SD memory card. Hereafter, the controller is also abbreviated as the SD controller. The following are the main features of the controller. 

 Interface with MCU by APB bus 

 16/32-bit access on APB bus 

 16/32-bit access for control registers 

 32-bit access for FIFO 

Built-in 32 bytes FIFO buffers for transmit and receive, FIFO is shared for transmit and receive 

 Built-in CRC circuit 

 CRC generation can be disabled 

 DMA supported 

 Interrupt capabilities 

 Data rate up to 48 Mbps in serial mode, 48x4 Mbps in parallel model, the module is targeted at 48 MHz operating clock 

 Serial clock rate on SD bus is programmable 

 Card detection capabilities during sleep mode 

 Controllability of power for memory card 

 Does not support SPI mode for SD memory card 

 Does not support multiple SD memory cards 

## 4.13.2 Overview

## 4.13.2.1 Pin Assignment

The following lists pins required for the SD memory card. Table 66 shows how the pins are shared. Note that all I/O pads have embedded both pull-up and pull-down resistors because they are shared by the SD memory card. The pull-down resistors for these pins can be used for power saving. If optimal pull-up or pull-down resistors are required on the system board, all embedded pull-up and pull-down resistors can be disabled by programming the corresponding control registers. The VDDPD pin is used for power saving. Power for the SD memory card can be shut down by programming the corresponding control register. The WP (Write Protection) pin is used to detect the status of the Write Protection Switch on the SD memory card.

## 4.13.2.2 Card Detection

For SD memory card, detection of card insertion/removal by hardware is supported. Because a pull-down resistor with about 470 K resistance which is impractical to embed in an I/O pad is needed on the signal CD/DAT3, and it has to be capable of being connected or disconnected dynamically onto the signal CD during initialization period, an additional I/O pad is needed to switch on/off the pull-down resistor on the system board. The card detection for SD memory card is shown in Figure 51. Before SD memory card is inserted or powered on, SW1 and SW2 must be opened for card detection on the host side. Meanwhile, the pull-down resistor R on the system board must attach onto the signal CD/DAT3 by the output signal RCDEN. In addition, the default position of SW3 on the card is closed. 

Upon insertion of the SD memory card, the signal CD/DAT3 will transit from low to high. If the SD memory card is removed, the CD/DAT3 signal will return to logic low state. After the card identification process, the pull-down resistor R on the system board shall be disconnected with the CD/DAT3 signal, and SW3 on the card must be opened for normal operation. 

Since the scheme above needs a mechanical switch such as a relay on the system board, it is not an ideal scheme. Thus, a dedicated pin “INS” is used to perform card insertion and removal for SD. The pin “INS” will be connected to the pin “VSS2” of a SD connector (see Figure 50). 

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-20/a0b5e3cf-5739-46d1-96ae-050c03350f29/9561305df1709650aa05517ada6a6ab4461d388c077dc756fac89ee390e80716.jpg)



Figure 50. Card detection for SD memory card (Scheme 1)

## 4.13.3.1 Global Register Definition


MSDC+0000h SD memory card controller configuration register MSDC_CFG


<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>31</td><td>30</td><td>29</td><td>28</td><td>27</td><td>26</td><td>25</td><td>24</td><td>23</td><td>22</td><td>21</td><td>20</td><td>19</td><td>18</td><td>17</td><td>16</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">FIFOTHD</td><td></td><td></td><td>VDDPD</td><td>RCDEN</td><td>DIRQEN</td><td>PINE N</td><td>DMA EN</td><td>INTE N</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">R/W</td><td></td><td></td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">0100</td><td></td><td></td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr></table>

<table><tr><td>Name</td><td>SCLKF</td><td>SCLKON</td><td>CRED</td><td>STDBY</td><td>CLKSRC</td><td>NOCRC</td><td>RST</td><td>MSDC</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>W</td><td>R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td>00000000</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>1</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td></tr></table>

The register is for general configuration of the SD controller. Note that MSDC_CFG[31:16] can be accessed by the 16-bit APB bus. 

## MSDC

Configures the controller for SD memory card mode operation. The CLK/CMD/DAT line is pulled low when the SD memory card mode is disabled. 

0 Disable SD memory card 

1 Enable SD memory card 

## NOCR C

Disables CRC. ‘1’ indicates that data transfer without CRC is desired. For the write data block, the data will be transmitted without CRC. For the read data block, the CRC will not be checked. because it is for testing purposes. 

0 Data transfer with CRC is desired. 

1 Data transfer without CRC is desired. 

RST Software seset. Writing ‘1’ to this register bit will cause an internal synchronous reset of the SD controller. However, this does not reset register settings. 

0 Otherwise 

1 Reset SD controller 

## CLKSRC

Specifies which clock is used as the source clock of the memory card. Use MPLL (780MHz) hopping clock as the source clock of the memory card. The fastest clock rate for the memory card is 97.5/2 = 48.75MHz when frequency hopping is enabled. 

00 Use MPLL clock divide by 8 as the source clock of the memory card. The source clock is 97.5MHz when MPLL is used. 

01 Use MPLL clock divide by 9 as the source clock of the memory card. The source clock is 86.6MHz when MPLL is used. 

10 Use MPLL clock divide by 10 as the source clock of the memory card. The source clock is 78MHz when MPLL is used. 

11 Use MPLL clock divide by 12 as the source clock of the memory card. The source clock is 65MHz when MPLL is used. 

## STDBY

Standby mode. If the module is powered down, the clock operation to the module will be stopped. At the same time, the clock to card detection circuitry will also be stopped. If detection of the memory card insertion and removal is desired, write ‘1’ to the register bit. If the interrupt for detection of the memory card insertion and removal is enabled, an interrupt will take place whenever the memory is inserted or removed. 

0 Standby mode is disabled. 

Standby mode is enabled. 

## CRED

Rising edge data. This register bit is used to determine the serial data input is latched at the falling edge or the rising edge of the serial clock. The default setting is at the rising edge. If the serial data have worse timing, set this register bit to ‘1’. When the memory card has worse timing on the return read data, set this register bit to ‘1’. 

0 Serial data input is latched at the rising edge of the serial clock. 

1 Serial data input is latched at the falling edge of the serial clock. 

SCLKON Serial clock always on. Used for debugging purposes. 

0 Serial clock not always on 

1 Serial clock always on 

## SCLKF

Controls clock frequency of serial clock on the SD bus. Denote the clock frequency of SD bus serial clock as $\mathsf { f } _ { \mathsf { s l a v e } }$ and clock frequency of the SD controller as $\boldsymbol { \mathrm { f } } _ { \mathrm { h o s t } }$ which is 97.5 MHz. The value of theisregister field is as the following. Note that the allowable maximum frequency of $f _ { s I a v e }$ is 48.75MHz. While changing the clock rate, it needs “ 1T clock period before change + 1T clock period after change” for hardware signal to re-synchronize. 

00000000b $\mathfrak { f } _ { \mathsf { s l a v e } } = ( 1 / 2 ) ^ { \star } \mathfrak { f } _ { \mathsf { h o s t } }$ 

00000001b $\mathfrak { f } _ { \mathsf { s l a v e } } = [ 1 / ( 4 ^ { \star } 1 ) ] ^ { \star } \mathfrak { f } _ { \mathsf { h o s t } }$ 

00000010b $\mathfrak { f } _ { \mathsf { s l a v e } } = [ 1 / ( 4 ^ { \star } 2 ) ] ^ { \star } \mathfrak { f } _ { \mathsf { h o s t } }$ 

00000011b $\mathfrak { f } _ { \mathsf { s l a v e } } = [ 1 / ( 4 ^ { \star } 3 ) ) ^ { \star } \mathfrak { f } _ { \mathsf { h o s t } }$ 

00010000b $\mathfrak { f } _ { \mathtt { s l a v e } } = [ 1 / ( 4 ^ { \star } 1 6 ) ] ^ { \star } \mathfrak { f } _ { \mathtt { h o s t } }$ 

11111111b $\mathfrak { f } _ { \mathsf { s l a v e } } = [ 1 / ( 4 ^ { \star } 2 5 5 ) ] ^ { \star } \mathfrak { f } _ { \mathsf { h o s t } }$ 

## INTEN

Enables interrupt. Note that if the interrupt capability is disabled, the application software must poll the status of the MSDC_STA register to check for any interrupt requests. 

0 Disable interrupt induced by various conditions 

1 Enables interrupt induced by various conditions 

## DMAEN

Enables DMA. Note that if DMA capability is disabled, the application software must poll the status of the $M S D C \_ S T A$ register for checking any data transfer requests. If DMA is desired, the register bit must be set before the command register is written. 

0 Disable DMA request induced by various conditions 

1 Enable DMA request induced by various conditions 

## PINEN

Enables pin interrupt. This register bit is used to determine if the pin for card detection is used as an interrupt source. 

0 The pin for card detection is not used as an interrupt source. 

1 The pin for card detection is used as an interrupt source. 

## DIRQEN

Enables data request interrupt. The register bit is used to determine if the data request is used as an interrupt source. 

0 Data request is not used as an interrupt source. 

1 Data request is used as an interrupt source. 

MediaTek Confidential 

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-20/a0b5e3cf-5739-46d1-96ae-050c03350f29/de87ac337db85b536addf36fecbae4f54fdf1ae1ec9d31c49e3f59a9dc0bb52e.jpg)


## RCDEN

Controls the output pin RCDEN used for the card identification process when the controller is for SD memory card. Its output will control the pull down resistor on the system board to connect or disconnect with the CD/DAT3 signal. 

0 The output pin RCDEN will output logic low. 

1 The output pin RCDEN will output logic high. 

## VDDPD

Controls the output pin VDDPD is used for power saving. The output pin VDDPD controls power for the memory card. 

0 The output pin VDDPD will output logic low. The power for the memory card will be turned off. 

1 The output pin VDDPD will output logic high. The power for the memory card will be turned on. 

## FIFOTHD

FIFO threshold. Determines when to issue a DMA request. For write transactions, DMA requests will be asserted if the number of free entries in FIFO are larger than or equal to the value in the register field. For read transactions, DMA requests will be asserted if the number of valid entries in FIFO are larger than or equal to the value in the register field. The register field must be set according to the setting of the data transfer count in DMA burst mode. If the single mode for DMA transfer is used, the register field shall be set to 0b0001. 

0000 Invalid 

0001 Threshold value is 1. 

0010 Threshold value is 2. 

1000 Threshold value is 8. 

Others Invalid 

## MSDC+0004h SD memory card controller status register

MSDC_STA 

<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td>BUSY</td><td>FIFO CLR</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">FIFOCNT</td><td>INT</td><td>DRQ</td><td>BE</td><td>BF</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td>R</td><td>W</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">RO</td><td>RO</td><td>RO</td><td>RO</td><td>RO</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td>0</td><td>-</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="4">0000</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td></tr></table>

The register contains the status of FIFO, interrupts and data requests. 

BF 

Indicates if FIFO in the SD controller is full 

0 FIFO in SD controller is not full. 

1 FIFO in SD controller is full. 

BE 

Indicates if FIFO in the SD controller is empty 

0 FIFO in SD controller is not empty. 

MediaTek Confidential 

## 1 FIFO in SD controller is empty.

## DRQ

Indicates if any data transfer is required. When any data transfer is required, this register bit will still be active even if register bit DIRQEN in register MSDC_CFG is disabled. Data transfer can be achieved by the DMA channel, thus alleviating MCU loading, or by polling the register bit to check if any data transfer is requested. When register bit DIRQEN in register MSDC_CFG is disabled, the second method is used. 

0 No DMA request exists. 

1 DMA request exists. 

## INT

Indicates if any interrupt exists. When any interrupt exists, the register bit will still be active even if register bit INTEN in register MSDC_CFG is disabled. The SD controller can interrupt MCU by issuing an interrupt request to the interrupt controller, or by the software/application endlessly polling the register to check for an interrupt request in the SD controller. While register bit INTEN in register MSDC_CFG is disabled, the latter method is used. For read commands, it is possible that a time-out may occur. The software can read the status register to check and see if the time-out error takes place without OS time tick support or an asserted data request. Note that the register bit will be cleared when reading register MSDC_INT. 

0 No interrupt request exists. 

1 Interrupt request exists. 

FIFOCNT FIFO count. Shows how many valid entries are in FIFO 

0000 There is 0 valid entry in FIFO. 

0001 There is 1 valid entry in FIFO. 

0001 There are 2 valid entries in FIFO. 

1000 There are 8 valid entries in FIFO. 

Others Invalid 

FIFOCLR Clears FIFO. Writing ‘1’ to the register bit will cause the content of FIFO to clear and reset the status of the FIFO controller. 

0 No effect on FIFO 

1 Clear the content of FIFO and reset the status of FIFO controller. 

## BUSY

Status of the controller. If the controller is in busy state, the register bit will be ‘1’. 

Otherwise it will be ‘0’. 

The controller is in idle state. 

1 The controller is in busy state. 

## MSDC+0008h SD memory card controller interrupt register

MSDC_INT 

<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr></table>

<table><tr><td>Name</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>SDIOIRQ</td><td>SDR1BIRQ</td><td></td><td>SDMCIRQ</td><td>SDDATIRQ</td><td>SDCMDIRQ</td><td>PINIRQ</td><td>DIRQ</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>RC</td><td>RC</td><td></td><td>RC</td><td>RC</td><td>RC</td><td>RC</td><td>RC</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td></td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td></tr></table>

The register contains the status of interrupts. Note that the register still shows the status of an interrupt even though the interrupt is disabled, that is, register bit INTEN of register MSDC_CFG is set to ‘0. It implies that a software interrupt can be implemented by polling register bit INT of register MSDC_STA and this register. However, if a hardware interrupt is desired, be sure to clear the register before setting register bit INTEN of register MSDC_CFG to ‘1’, or undesired hardware interrupt may occur from the previous interrupt status. 

## DIRQ

Data request interrupt. Indicates if an interrupt for data request exists. Whenever a data request exists, and the data request as an interrupt source is enabled, i.e. register bit DIRQEN in register MSDC_CFG is set to ‘1’, the register bit will be active. It will be reset upon being read. For software, the data requests can be recognized by polling register bit DRQ or by a data request interrupt. Data request interrupts will be generated with all FIFOTHD data transfers. 

0 No data request interrupt 

1 Data request interrupt occurs. 

## PINIRQ

Pin change interrupt. Indicates if an interrupt for the memory card insertion/remova exists. Whenever a memory card is inserted or removed and the card detection interrupt is enabled, i.e. register bit PINEN in register MSDC_CFG is set to ‘1’, the register bit will be set to ‘1’. It will be reset when the register is read. 

0 Otherwise 

1 Card is inserted or removed. 

## SDCMDIRQ

SD bus CMD interrupt. Indicates if an interrupt for the SD CMD line exists. Whenever an interrupt for the SD CMD line exists, i.e. any bit in register SDC_CMDSTA is active, the register bit will be set to ‘1’ if the interrupt is enabled. It will be reset when the register is read. 

0 No SD CMD line interrupt 

SD CMD line interrupt exists. 

## SDDATIRQ

SD bus DAT interrupt. Indicates if an interrupt for the SD DAT line exists. Whenever an interrupt for the SD DAT line exists, i.e. any bit in register SDC_ DATSTA is active, the register bit will be set to ‘1’ if the interrupt is enabled. It will be reset when the register is read. 

0 No SD DAT line interrupt 

1 SD DAT line interrupt exists. 

## SDMCIRQ

SD memory card interrupt. Indicates if an interrupt for the SD memory card exists. Whenever an interrupt for the SD memory card exists, i.e. any bit in register 

SDC_CSTA is active, the register bit will be set to ‘1’ if the interrupt is enabled. It will be reset when the register is read. Note that this interrupt will not call interrupt process program. 

0 No SD memory card interrupt 

1 SD memory card interrupt exists. 

## SDR1BIRQ

SD R1b response interrupt. Active when a SD command with R1b response finishes and the DAT0 line has transited from busy to idle state. Single block write commands with R1b response will cause the interrupt when the command completes no matter whether it is successful or a CRC error has occurred. However, multi-block write commands with R1b response do not cause the interrupt because multi-block write commands are always stopped by STOP_TRANS commands. STOP_TRANS commands (with R1b response) behind multi-block write commands will cause the interrupt. Single block read command with R1b response will cause the interrupt when the command completes but multi-block read commands will not. Note that STOP_TRANS commands (with R1b response) behind multi-block read commands will cause the interrupt. 

0 No interrupt for SD R1b response 

1 Interrupt for SD R1b response exists. 

## SDIOIRQ

SDIO interrupt. Indicates if there is an SDIO interrupt on data line 1/5 (selected by SDIO_CFG.INTSEL). When in the 1-bit data line mode, DAT1 is dedicated for SDIO interrupt, so in this mode when DAT1 goes low, SDIOIRQ will be active unless this interrupt is masked till read-clear. When in the 4-bit data line mode, if DAT1 goes low during the interrupt period, SDIOIRQ will be active unless this interrupt is masked till read-clear. 

0 No interrupt for SDIO interrupt 

1 Interrupt for SDIO interrupt 

## MSDC+000C

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-20/a0b5e3cf-5739-46d1-96ae-050c03350f29/8d4f2249155aacc32ee58d12f6e303c3b934dd6caeb870f7f6afb6aa9fda612f.jpg)



h



SD memory card pin status register



MSDC_PS


<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>31</td><td>30</td><td>29</td><td>28</td><td>27</td><td>26</td><td>25</td><td>24</td><td>23</td><td>22</td><td>21</td><td>20</td><td>19</td><td>18</td><td>17</td><td>16</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>CMD</td><td colspan="8">DAT</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>RO</td><td colspan="8">RO</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>-</td><td colspan="8">-</td></tr><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td colspan="4">CDDEBOUNCE</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>PINCHG</td><td>PIN0</td><td>POEN0</td><td>PIEN0</td><td>CDE N</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td colspan="4">RW</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>RC</td><td>RO</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td colspan="4">0000</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>0</td><td>1</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td></tr></table>

The register is used for card detection. When the memory card controller is powered on, and the system is powered on, the power for the memory card remains off unless the power has been supplied by the PMIC. Meanwhile, the pad for card detection defaults to pull down when the system is powered on. 

For detecting card insertion, first pull up the INS pin, then enable the card detection and input pin at the same time. After 32 cycles of controller clock, the status of pin changes will emerge. For detecting card removal, simply keep enabling card detection and the input pin. 

CDEN Enables card detection. Enables or disables card detection. 

0 Disable card detection 

1 Enable card detection 

PIEN0 Controls input pin for card detection 

0 Disable input pin for card detection 

1 Enable input pin for card detection 

POEN0 Controls output of input pin for card detection. 

0 Disable output of input pin for card detection 

1 Enable output of input pin for card detection 

PIN0 Value of input pin for card detection 

0 The value of input pin for card detection is logic low. 

1 The value of input pin for card detection is logic high. 

PINCHG Pin change. Indicates the status of card insertion/removal. If the memory card is inserted or removed, the register bit will be set to ‘1’ no matter if the pin change interrupt is enabled or not. It will be cleared when the register is read. 

0 Otherwise 

1 Card is inserted or removed. 

## CDDEBOUNCE

Specifies the time interval for card detection de-bounce. Its default value is 0. It means that the de-bounce interval is 32 cycle times of 32kHz. The interval will extend by one 32kHz cycle time by increasing the counter by 1. 

DAT Memory card data lines 

CMD Memory card command lines

MSDC_DAT

MSDC+0014h SD memory card controller IO control register


<table><tr><td>Type</td><td>R/W</td></tr></table>

The register is used to read/write data from/to FIFO inside the SD controller. Data access unit: 32 bits. 

MSDC_IOCON 

<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>31</td><td>30</td><td>29</td><td>28</td><td>27</td><td>26</td><td>25</td><td>24</td><td>23</td><td>22</td><td>21</td><td>20</td><td>19</td><td>18</td><td>17</td><td>16</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2">SAMPLEDLY</td><td colspan="2">FIXDLY</td><td>SAMPON</td><td>CRCDIS</td><td>CMDSEL</td><td colspan="2">INTLH</td><td>DSW</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2">R/W</td><td colspan="2">R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td colspan="2">R/W</td><td>R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="2">01</td><td colspan="2">00</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td colspan="2">0</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td>CMDRE</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>HIGH_SPEED</td><td colspan="2">DMABURST</td><td>SRCFG1</td><td>SRCFG0</td><td colspan="3">ODCCFG1</td><td colspan="3">ODCCFG0</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td>R/W</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>R/W</td><td colspan="2">R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td colspan="3">R/W</td><td colspan="3">R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td>0</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>0</td><td colspan="2">00</td><td>1</td><td>1</td><td colspan="3">000</td><td colspan="3">011</td></tr></table>

The register specifies the output driving capability and slew rate of IO pads for MSDC. The reset value is the suggested setting. If the output driving capability of pins DAT0, DAT1, DAT2 and DAT3 is too large, it is possible to raise the ground bounce and thus result in glitch on SCLK. The actual driving current will depend on the PAD type selected for the chip. 

ODCCFG0 Output driving capability of pins CMD/BS and SCLK 000 4mA 001 8mA 010 12mA 011 16mA 

ODCCFG1 Output driving capability of pins DAT0, DAT1, DAT2 and DAT3 000 4mA 001 8mA 010 12mA 011 16mA 

SRCFG0 Output driving capability of pins CMD/BS and SCLK Fast slew rate Slow slew rate 

Output driving capability of pins DAT0, DAT1, DAT2 and DAT3 0 Fast slew rate 1 Slow slew rate 

## CMDRE

Determines whether or not the host should latch a response token (sent from he card on the CMD line ) at the rising edge or falling edge of the serial clock 

0 Host latches response at the rising edge of the serial clock 

1 Host latches response at the falling edge of the serial clock 

## DSW

Determines whether or not the host should latch data with 1-T delay. For the SD card, 

this bit is suggest to be 0. 

0 Host latches the data with 1-T delay 

1 Host latches the data without 1-T delay 

## INTLH

Selects the latch timing for SDIO multi-block read interrupt. 

00 Host latches INT at the second backend clock after the end bit of the current data block from the card is received. (This is the default setting) 

01 Host latches INT at the first backend clock after the end bit of the current data block from the card is received. 

10 Host latches INT at the second backend clock after the end bit of the current data block from the card is received. 

11 Host latches INT at the third backend clock after the end bit of the current data block from the card is received. 

## CMDSEL

Determines whether or not the host should delay 1-T to latch response from the card. 

0 Host latches response without 1-T delay 

1 Host latches response with 1-T delay 

## CRCDIS

Switches off the data CRC check for SD read data. 

0 CRC check is on. 

1 CRC check is off. 

## HIGH_SPEED

For high-speed mode when multiple phase clock is used. High-speed mode means that the SD bus serial clock rate is bigger than 25MHz. The default speed mode means that the SD bus serial clock rate bigger than 25MHz. 

0 Default speed 

1 High speed 

## DMABURST

Used for software to select the burst type when the data is transferred by DMA. Note that only single mode operation can support non-4N bytes data transfer in read operation. 

00 Single mode 

01 4-beat incrementing burst 

10 8-beat incrementing burst 

11 Reserved 

## SAMPON

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-20/a0b5e3cf-5739-46d1-96ae-050c03350f29/af06fd8ae74f7e3b2ae87bebe2928d852b215f147c4d187bf54c8894bf54457b.jpg)


Data sample enable always on. This bit is suggested to be set to “1” when feedback clock is used and “0” when multiple phase clock is used. 

0 Data sample enable not always on 

## 1 Data sample enable always on

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-20/a0b5e3cf-5739-46d1-96ae-050c03350f29/84331323f2d63dec3f40f80edf0ca41386d76d1a651b96064389acff4bcf4131.jpg)


## FIXDLY

Used for software to select the delay cycle after the clock fixes high for the host controller to SD card. 

00 0-T delay 

01 1-T delay 

10 2-T delay 

11 3-T delay 

SAMPLEDLY Used for software to select the turn around delay cycle between the write data end bit and CRC status for SD card. 

00 0-T delay 

01 1-T delay 

10 2-T delay 

11 3-T delay 


MSDC+0018h SD memory card controller IO control register1

Pull-up/down register value for pin RST/WP. Default value: 10

10 Pull-up/down resistor in the I/O pad of pin WP value is 47k. 

11 Pull-up/down resistor in the I/O pad of pin WP value is 23.5k.

## 4.13.3.2 SD Memory Card Controller Register Definition

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-20/a0b5e3cf-5739-46d1-96ae-050c03350f29/abca6fcb42c483392c1b1bf5e7e41ca9e964b1ce1cecf7dd5146f68d966fbfb3.jpg)



MSDC+0020h SD memory card controller configuration register



SDC_CFG


<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>31</td><td>30</td><td>29</td><td>28</td><td>27</td><td>26</td><td>25</td><td>24</td><td>23</td><td>22</td><td>21</td><td>20</td><td>19</td><td>18</td><td>17</td><td>16</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td colspan="8">DTOC</td><td colspan="4">WDOD</td><td>SDIO</td><td></td><td>MDLEN</td><td>SIEN</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td colspan="8">R/W</td><td colspan="4">R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td></td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td colspan="8">00000000</td><td colspan="4">0000</td><td>0</td><td></td><td>0</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td colspan="4">BSYDLY</td><td colspan="12">BLKLEN</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td colspan="4">R/W</td><td colspan="12">R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td colspan="4">1000</td><td colspan="12">00000000000</td></tr></table>

The register is used for configuring the SD memory card controller when it is configured as the host of SD memory card. Note that SDC_CFG[31:16] can be accessed by the 16-bit APB bus. 

BLKLEN Block length. Defines the length (in bytes) of one block of a data transaction. Maximum value of block length: 2,048 bytes 

000000000000 Reserved 

000000000001 1 byte 

000000000010 2 bytes 

011111111111 2,047 bytes 

100000000000 2,048 bytes 

BSYDLY Only valid for commands with R1b response. If the command has a response of R1b type, the SD controller must monitor the data line 0 for card busy status from the bit time that is two serial clock cycles after the command end bit to check if the operation in the SD memory card have finished. This register field is used to expand the time between the command end bit and end of detection period to detect card busy status. If the time is up, and there is no card busy status on data line 0, the controller will abandon the detection. 

0000 No extension 

0001 Extend 1 more serial clock cycle 

0010 Extend 2 more serial clock cycles 

1111 Extend 15 more serial clock cycles 

## SIEN

Enables serial interface. Should be enabled as soon as possible before any command. 

0 Disable serial interface for SD 

1 Enable serial interface for SD 

## SDIO

Enables SDIO 

0 Disable SDIO mode 

1 Enable SDIO mode 

MediaTek Confidential 

## MDLEN

Enables multiple data line. Can be enabled only when the SD memory card is applied and detected by software application. 

0 Disable 4-bit data line 

1 Enable 4-bit data line 

## WDOD

Write data output delay. The period from finish of the response for the initial host write command or the last write data block in a multiple block write operation to the start bit of the next write data block requires at least two serial clock cycles. The register field is used to extend the period (Write Data Output Delay) Unit: One serial clock 

0000 No extension 

0001 Extend 1 more serial clock cycle 

0010 Extend 2 more serial clock cycles 

1111 Extend 15 more serial clock cycles 

## DTOC

Data time-out counter. The period from finish of the initial host read command or the last read data block in a multiple block read operation to the start bit of the next read data block requires at least two serial clock cycles. The counter is used to extend the period (Read Data Access Time) Unit: 65,536 serial clock. Refer to descriptions of register bit RDINT. 

00000000 Extend 65,536 more serial clock cycles 

00000001 Extend 65,536x2 more serial clock cycles 

00000010 Extend 65,536x3 more serial clock cycles 

11111111 Extend 65,536x 256 more serial clock cycles 


MSDC+0024h SD memory card controller command register



SDC_CMD


<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>31</td><td>30</td><td>29</td><td>28</td><td>27</td><td>26</td><td>25</td><td>24</td><td>23</td><td>22</td><td>21</td><td>20</td><td>19</td><td>18</td><td>17</td><td>16</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>CMD FAIL</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td></td><td>STOP</td><td>RW</td><td colspan="2">DTYPE</td><td>IDRT</td><td colspan="3">RSPTYP</td><td></td><td colspan="6">CMD</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td></td><td>R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td colspan="2">R/W</td><td>R/W</td><td colspan="3">R/W</td><td></td><td colspan="6">R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td></td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td colspan="2">00</td><td>0</td><td colspan="3">000</td><td></td><td colspan="6">000000</td></tr></table>

The register defines a SD memory card command and its attribute. Before the SD controller issues a transaction onto SD bus, application shall specify other relative settings such as argument for command. After the application writes the register, the SD controller will issue the corresponding transaction onto SD serial bus. If the command is GO_IDLE_STATE, the controller will have serial clock on the SD bus run 128 cycles before issuing the command. 

CMD SD memory card command. Total 6 bits. 

## RSPTYP

Defines response type for the command. For commands with R1 and R1b response, register SDC_CSTA (not SDC_STA) will update after response token is received. Register SDC_CSTA contains the status of the SD and it will be used as response interrupt sources. Note that if CMD7 is used with all 0’s RCA then RSPTYP must be “000”. Command “GO_TO_IDLE” also have RSPTYP = ’000’. 

000 No response for the command. For instance, broadcast command without response and GO_INACTIVE_STATE command. 

001 The command has R1 response. R1 response token is 48-bit. 

010 The command has R2 response. R2 response token is 136-bit. 

011 The command has R3 response. Even though R3 is 48-bit response, but it does not contain CRC checksum. 

100 Reserved. 

101 Reserved. 

110 The command has R6 response. R6 response token is 48-bit. 

111 The command has R1b response. If the command has a response of R1b type, SD controller must monitor the data line 0 for card busy status from the bit time that is two or four serial clock cycles after the command end bit to check if the operation in the SD memory card is finished. There are two cases for detecting the card busy status. The first case is that the host stops the data transmission during an active write data transfer. The card will assert busy signal after the stop transmission command end bit followed by four serial clock cycles. The second case is that the card is in the idle state or under a scenario of receiving a stop transmission command between data blocks when multiple block write command is in progress. The register bit is valid only when the command has a response token. 

## IDRT

Identification response time. The register bit indicates if the command has a response with N (i.e. 5 serial clock cycles as defined in SD Memory Card Specification Part 1 Physical Layer Specification version 1.0) response time. The register bit is valid only when the command has a response token. Thus the register bit must be set to ‘1’ for CMD2 (ALL_SEND_CID) and ACMD41 (SD_APP_OP_CMD). 

0 Otherwise 

1 The command has a response with $\mathsf { N } _ { \mathsf { I D } }$ response time. 

## DTYPE

Defines data token type for the command 

00 No data token for the command 

01 Single block transaction 

10 Multiple block transaction, i.e. the command is a multiple-block read or write command. 

11 Reserved. 

## RW

Defines the command is a read command or write command. The register bit is valid only when the command will cause a transaction with data token. 

0 The command is a read command. 

1 The command is a write command. 

## STOP

Indicates if the command is a stop transmission command. 

0 The command is not a stop transmission command. 

1 The command is a stop transmission command. 


MSDC+0028h SD memory card controller argument register



SDC_ARG


<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>31</td><td>30</td><td>29</td><td>28</td><td>27</td><td>26</td><td>25</td><td>24</td><td>23</td><td>22</td><td>21</td><td>20</td><td>19</td><td>18</td><td>17</td><td>16</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td colspan="16">ARG [31:16]</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td colspan="16">R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td colspan="16">ARG [15:0]</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td colspan="16">R/W</td></tr></table>


The register contains the argument of the SD memory card command. 


## MSDC+002C


h



SD memory card controller status register



SDC_STA


<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td>WP</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>FEDATBUSY</td><td>FECMDBUSY</td><td>BEDATBUSY</td><td>BECMDBUSY</td><td>BESDCBUSY</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td>R</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>RO</td><td>RO</td><td>RO</td><td>RO</td><td>RO</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td>-</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td></tr></table>

The register contains various status of the SD controller as the controller is configured as the host of the SD memory card. 

## BESDCBUSY

Indicates if the SD controller is busy, i.e. any transmission is going on CMD or DAT line on the SD bus. This bit shows backend controller’s SDC busy state. The busy state is sync from card clock domain to bus clock domain. 

0 Backend SD controller is idle. 

Backend SD controller is busy. 

## BECMDBUSY

Indicates if any transmission is going on CMD line on the SD bus. This bit shows backend controller’s CMD busy state. The busy state is sync from the card clock domain to bus clock domain. 

0 Backend SDC controller gets the info that no transmission is going on CMD line on the SD bus. 

1 Backend SDC controller gets the info that there exists transmission going on CMD line on the SD bus. 

## BEDATBUSY

Indicates if any transmission is going on DAT line on the SD bus 

0 Backend SDC controller gets the info that no transmission is going on DAT line on SD the bus. 

1 Backend SDC controller gets the info that there exists transmission going on DAT line on the SD bus. 

## FECCMDBUSY

Indicates if any transmission is going on CMD line on the SD bus. This bit indicates directly the CMD line at the card clock domain. 

0 No transmission is going on CMD line on the SD bus. 

1 There exists transmission going on CMD line on the SD bus. 

## FEDATBUSY

Indicates if any transmission is going on DAT line on the SD bus. This bit indicates directly the CMD line at the card clock domain. For those commands without data but still involving DAT line, the register bit is useless. For example, if an Erase command is issued, checking if the register bit is ‘0’ before issuing the next command with data will not guarantee the controller is idle. In this situation, use register bit BESDCBUSY. 

0 No transmission is going on DAT line on the SD bus. 

1 There exists transmission going on DAT line on the SD bus. 

## WP

Detects the status of Write Protection Switch on the SD memory card. The register bit shows the status of Write Protection Switch on the SD memory card. There is no default reset value. The WP (Write Protection) pin is also only useful when the controller is configured for the SD memory card. 

0 Write Protection Switch off. It means the memory card is writable. 

1 Write Protection Switch on. It means the memory card is desired to be write-protected.

## MSDC+003C

<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>31</td><td>30</td><td>29</td><td>28</td><td>27</td><td>26</td><td>25</td><td>24</td><td>23</td><td>22</td><td>21</td><td>20</td><td>19</td><td>18</td><td>17</td><td>16</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td colspan="16">RESP [127:112]</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td colspan="16">RO</td></tr><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td colspan="16">RESP [111:96]</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td colspan="16">RO</td></tr></table>

The register contains parts of the last SD memory card bus response. Register fields SDC_RESP0, SDC_RESP1, SDC_RESP2 and SDC_RESP3 compose the last SD memory card bus response. For response of type R2, i.e. response of the command ALL_SEND_CID, SEND_CSD and SEND_CID, only bit 127 to 0 of response token are stored in register field SDC_RESP0, SDC_RESP1, SDC_RESP2 and SDC_RESP3. For response of other types, only bit 39 to 8 of response token are stored in register field SDC_RESP0. 

![image](https://cdn-mineru.openxlab.org.cn/result/2026-09-20/a0b5e3cf-5739-46d1-96ae-050c03350f29/41fdc67bb4d90539c97b1b262ecc07a9e445d71c468021da1cc8976a45d0e79f.jpg)


## SD memory card controller command status MSDC+0040h register

## SDC_CMDSTA

<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>RSPC RCE RR</td><td>CMD TO</td><td>CMD RDY</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>RC</td><td>RC</td><td>RC</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td>0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td></tr></table>

The register contains the status of SD controller during command execution and that of SD bus protocol after command execution when SD controller is configured as the host of SD memory card. The register will also be used as interrupt sources. The register will be cleared when being read. Meanwhile, if the interrupt is enabled and thus interrupt caused by the register is generated, reading the register will deassert the interrupt. 

## CMDRDY

For command without response, the register bit will be ‘1’ once the command is completed on the SD bus. For command with response, the register bit will be ‘1’ whenever the command is issued onto SD bus and its corresponding response is received without CRC error. 

0 Otherwise 

1 Command with/without response finish successfully without CRC error. 

## CMDTO

Time-out on CMD detected. ‘1’ indicates that SD controller detected a timeout condition while waiting for a response on the CMD line. 

0 Otherwise 

1 SD controller detected a timeout condition while waiting for a response on the CMD line. 

## RSPCRCERR

CRC error on CMD detected. ‘1’ indicates that the SD controller detected a CRC error after reading a response from the CMD line. The register will also update in the clock auto calibration mode. After clock auto calibration, the software should read and clear the register. 

0 Otherwise 

1 SD controller detected a CRC error after reading a response from the CMD line. 


MSDC+0044h SD memory card controller data status register SDC_DATSTA


<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="8">DATCRCERR</td><td>DATTO</td><td>BLKDONE</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="8">RC</td><td>RC</td><td>RC</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td></td><td colspan="8">0</td><td>0</td><td>0</td></tr></table>

The register contains the status of SD controller during data transfer on DAT line(s) when the SD controller is configured as the host of SD memory card. The register also will be used as interrupt sources. The register will be cleared when being read. Meanwhile, if interrupt is enabled and thus interrupt caused by the register is generated, reading the register will deassert the interrupt. 

## BLKDONE

Indicates the status of data block transfer 

0 Otherwise. 

1 A data block is successfully transferred. 

## DATTO

Time-out on DAT detected. ‘1’ indicates that SD controller detected a timeout condition while waiting for data token on the DAT line. 

0 Otherwise 

1 SD controller detected a timeout condition while waiting for data token on the DAT line. 

## DATCRCERR[n]

CRC error on DAT detected. ‘1’ indicates that SD controller detected a CRC error for bit n after reading a block of data from the DAT line or SD signaled a CRC error after writing a block of data to the DAT line. The register will also update in the clock auto calibration mode. After clock auto calibration, the software should read and clear the register. 

0 Otherwise 

1 SD controller detected a CRC error after reading a block of data from the DAT line or SD signaled a CRC error after writing a block of data to the DAT line. 

Note: n is 7~0 for 8-bit mode. Each bit is read and cleared individually. 


MSDC+0048h SD memory card status register



SDC_CSTA


<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>31</td><td>30</td><td>29</td><td>28</td><td>27</td><td>26</td><td>25</td><td>24</td><td>23</td><td>22</td><td>21</td><td>20</td><td>19</td><td>18</td><td>17</td><td>16</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td colspan="16">CSTA [31:16]</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td colspan="16">RC</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td colspan="16">00000000000000000</td></tr><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td colspan="16">CSTA [15:0]</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td colspan="16">RC</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td colspan="16">00000000000000000</td></tr></table>

After commands with R1 and R1b response this register contains the status of the SD card, and it will be used as response interrupt sources. In all register fields, logic high indicates error, and logic low indicates there is no error. The register will be cleared when being read. Meanwhile, if the interrupt is enabled and thus interrupt caused by the register is generated, reading the register will deassert the interrupt. 

CSTA31 

OUT_OF_RANGE. The command’s argument is out of the allowed range for this card. 

CSTA30 ADDRESS_ERROR. A misaligned address that does not match the block length is used in the command. 

CSTA29 BLOCK_LEN_ERROR. The transferred block length is not allowed for this card, or the number of transferred bytes does not match the block length. 

CSTA28 ERASE_SEQ_ERROR. An error in the sequence of erase commands occurs. 

CSTA27 ERASE_PARAM. An invalid selection of write-blocks for erase occurs. 

CSTA26 WP_VIOLATION. Attempt to program a write-protected block. 

CSTA25 Reserved. Returns to zero. 

CSTA24 LOCK_UNLOCK_FAILED. Set when a sequence or password error has been detected in lock/unlock card command or if there is an attempt to access a locked card. 

CSTA23 COM_CRC_ERROR. CRC check of the previous failed command. 

CSTA22 ILLEGAL_COMMAND. Command not legal for the card state. 

CSTA21 CARD_ECC_FAILED. Card internal ECC is applied but fails to correct the data. 

CSTA20 CC_ERROR. Internal card controller error. 

CSTA19 ERROR. A general or unknown error occurs during the operation. 

CSTA18 UNDERRUN. The card cannot sustain data transfer in stream read mode. 

CSTA17 OVERRUN. The card cannot sustain data programming in stream write mode. 

CSTA16 CID/CSD_OVERWRITE. It can be either one of the following errors: 1) The CID register has already been written and cannot be overwritten. 2) The read-only section of CSD does not match the card. 3) An attempt to reverse the copy (set as original) or permanent WP (unprotected) bits is made. 

CSTA[15:4] Reserved. Returns to zero. 

CSTA3 AKE_SEQ_ERROR. Error in the sequence of authentication process 

CSTA[2:0] Reserved. Returns to zero.

K1


The register contains parts of the SD memory card interrupt mask register. Refer to the register descriptions of register SDC_IRQMASK1. The register will mask interrupt sources from register SDC_DATSTA, Note that IRQMASK0[6] will mask the SDC_DATSTA[9:2] together.‘1’ in some bits of the register will mask the corresponding interrupt source with the same bit position. For example, if IRQMASK[0] is ‘1’ then interrupt source from register field CMDRDY of register SDC_ CMDSTA will be masked. ‘0’ in some bits will not cause interrupt mask on the corresponding interrupt source from register SDC_CMDSTA and SDC_DATSTA. 


MSDC+0050h SD memory card IRQ mask register 1


SDC_IRQMAS 

<table><tr><td>Bit</td><td>31</td><td>30</td><td>29</td><td>28</td><td>27</td><td>26</td><td>25</td><td>24</td><td>23</td><td>22</td><td>21</td><td>20</td><td>19</td><td>18</td><td>17</td><td>16</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td colspan="16">IRQMASK [63:48]</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td colspan="16">R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td colspan="16">00000000000000000</td></tr><tr><td>Bit</td><td>15</td><td>14</td><td>13</td><td>12</td><td>11</td><td>10</td><td>9</td><td>8</td><td>7</td><td>6</td><td>5</td><td>4</td><td>3</td><td>2</td><td>1</td><td>0</td></tr><tr><td>Name</td><td colspan="16">IRQMASK [47:32]</td></tr><tr><td>Type</td><td colspan="16">R/W</td></tr><tr><td>Reset</td><td colspan="16">00000000000000000</td></tr></table>

The register contains parts of the SD memory card interrupt mask register. Registers SDC_IRQMASK1 and SDC_IRQMASK0 compose the SD memory card interrupt mask register. The register will mask interrupt sources from register SDC_CSTA. ‘1’ in some bits of the register will mask the corresponding interrupt source with the same bit position. For example, if IRQMASK[63] is ‘1’ then interrupt source from register field OUT_OF_RANGE of register SDC_ CSTA will be masked. ‘0’ in some bits will not cause interrupt mask on the corresponding interrupt source from register SDC_ CSTA.

The register is used to configure functionality for SDIO. 


## INTEN

Enables interrupt for SDIO 

0 Disable 

1 Enable 

INTSEL Selects interrupt signal 

0 Use data line 1 as interrupt signal 

1 Use data line 5 as interrupt signal 

DSBSEL Selects data block start bit just for SDIO enble mode. If 1-bit mode is used, set it to 0. 

0 Use data line 0 as start bit of data block and other data lines are ignored. 

1 Start bit of a data block is received only when data line 0-3 all become low.

This register is used to identify if thers is SDIO interrupt during the interrupt period on data line. 

IRQ SDIO interrupt exists on the data line. For example, during the interrupt period or in the 1-bit data line mode and DAT1/5 goes low from high, this bit will become 1 from 0. If DAT1/5 goes high from low, this bit will become 0 from 1. 

0 There is no SDIO interrupt existing on the data line. 

1 There is SDIO interrupt existing on the data line.

## CLKPAD_RED

Determines the input data from card is latched at the falling edge or rising edge of the feedback clock from pad. The suggested setting is “1” for the SD serial clock less than 25MHz and “0” for the SD serial clock higher than 25MHz. The suggested setting is “0” for MMC card no matter the serial clock rate is high speed or default speed. 

0 Rising edge to latch data 

1 Falling edge to latch data 

## 4.13.4 Application Notes

## 4.13.4.1 Initialization Procedures After Power-on

Disable power down control for MSDC module. Be sure to power on the MSDC module before starting any operation to it. 

## 4.13.4.2 Card Detection Procedures

The pseudo code is as the followinh: 

```txt
MSDC_CFG.PRCFG0 = 2'b10
MSDC_PS = 2'b11
MSDC_CFG.VDDPD = 1
if(MSDC_PS.PINCHG) { // card is inserted
    ... }
} 
```

The pseudo code segment performs the following tasks: 

1. First pull up the CD/DAT3 (INS) pin. 

2. Enable card detection and input pin at the same time. 

3. Turn on the power for memory card. 

4. Detect insertion of memory card. 

## 4.13.4.3 Notes On Commands

For SD, if the command desired to be issued involves data line, for example, commands with data transfer or R1b response, check if SDC_STA.SDCBUSY is ‘0’ before issuing. If the command desired to be issued does not involve data line, only check if SDC_STA.CMDBUSY is ‘0’ before issuing. 

## 4.13.4.4 Notes On Data Transfer

 For SD, if multiple-block-write command is issued then only issue the STOP_TRANS command inter-blocks instead of intra-blocks. 

 Once the software decides to issue STOP_TRANS commands, there should be no more data transferred from or to the controller. 

## 4.13.4.5 Notes On Frequency Change

Before changing the frequency of serial clock on SD bus, it is necessary to disable the serial interface of the controller. That is, set register bit SIEN of register SDC_CFG to ‘0’ for SD controller. The serial interface of the controller needs to be enabled again before starting any operation to the memory card. 

## 4.13.4.6 Notes On Response Time-out

If a read command doest not receive response, that is, it is terminated with a time-out, then register SDC_DATSTA needs to be cleared by reading it. Register bit “DATTO” should be active. However, it may take a while before the register bit becomes active. The alternative is to send the STOP_TRANS command. However, this method will receive a response with illegal-command information. Moreover, be sure to check if register bit SDC_STA.CMDBUSY is active before issuing the STOP_TRANS command. The procedure is as the following: 

1. Read command => Response time-out 

2. Issue STOP_TRANS command => Get response 

3. Read register SDC_DATSTA to clear it. 

## 4.13.4.7 Source or Destination Address Not Word-aligned

It is possible that the source address is not word-aligned when the data are moved from the memory to MSDC. Similarly, the destination address may be not word-aligned when the data are moved from MSDC to the memory. This can be solved by setting up the DMA byte-to-word function. 

1. $\mathsf { D M A n \_ C O N } . \mathsf { S l Z E } = 0$ 

2. $\mathsf { D M A n \_ C O N . B T W } = 1$ 

3. DMAn_CON.BURST = 2 (or 4) 

4. DMAn_COUNT = byte number instead of word number 

5. FIFO threshold setting must be 1 (or 2), depending on DMAn_CON.BURST 

Note: $n = 4 \sim 1 1$ 

## 4.13.4.8 Miscellaneous Notes

## Siemens MMC card

When a write command is issued and followed by a STOP_TRANS command, Siemens MMC card will de-assert busy status even though the flash programming has not yet been finished. The software must use “Get Status” command to make sure the flash programming is finished. 

## 4.14 BTIF

## 4.14.1 General Description

Bluetooth Interface (BTIF) is designed in SOC (BT+GSM) in order to be instead of the UART interface between the BT chip and the baseband chip. As in the UART design, BTIF is an APB slave which transmits or receives data by MCU access or through DMA/VFIFO.

Figure 52. Interface connection between BT and Baseband system

IIR Identifies if there are pending interrupts. The following table lists the IIR[5:0] codes associated with the possible interrupts:

Table 68. IIR[5:0] codes associated with the possible interrupts


Rx data received interrupt 

placed in the Rx buffer register. The interrupt is cleared by reading the Rx buffer register. 

## Rx data time-out interrupt

The Rx data time-out interrupt will be generated if all of the following conditions are applied: 

1. Rx buffer is empty. 

2. The most recent character is received longer than (RTOCNT*bclk period*4). 

3. RXFEN (IER[0]) is set to 1. 

The time-out timer is restarted upon receipt of a new byte from the Rx shift register. This interrupt is only valid while VFIFO is used. This register is cleared by reading the VFIFO status register (0x4C). 

## Tx holding register empty

A Tx holding register empty interrupt (IIR[3:0] = 0x02) is generated when TXEEN(IER[1]) is set and no data are placed in the Tx holding register. This interrupt is cleared by writing data into BTIF_THR (0x00).

## RX_DMA_EN

RX_DMA mechanism enable signal 

0 Does not use DMA in Rx. 

1 Use DMA in Rx. When this register is enabled, the flow control is based on the DMA threshold and generates a time-out interrupt 

## TX_DMA_EN

TX_DMA mechanism enable signal 

0 Does not use DMA in Tx. 

1 Use DMA in Tx. When this register is enabled, the flow control is based on the DMA threshold and generates a time-out interrupt for DMA. 

TO_CNT_AUTORST Time-out counter auto reset register 

0 After Rx time-out takes place, the software shall reset the interrupt by reading BTIF 0x4C. 

1 The time-out counter will be auto reset.
